An observer-specific rendering can vary lawful detail under the same world history; it cannot supply privately known inner motives or make the actor react to a silent internal visitor profile.

The real user remains a person beyond the game. The service may possess evidence about what the user *did in this experience*, and may make bounded, uncertain hypotheses that assist comprehension, accessibility or optional reflection. Those hypotheses are not direct knowledge of the human's hidden thoughts or diagnostic personality, nor are they automatically accessible to Nadia. Her model of the visitor may be more mistaken than the service's; the service may also be mistaken about the user. The psychological seriousness of the game depends on allowing these errors without collapsing them.

There is a tempting equation between better user models and better experiences. NEIGHBOR must resist it where “better” secretly means stronger persuasion, more compelling props, preselected intimacy, curated agreement or manufactured fear. A theory of irreducible people has been betrayed if it makes the human visitor exhaustible to a psychographic optimization system.

## 6. Her day does not wait for a session to begin or end

**Historical hypercompression, high-intensity possibility and ordinary continuation are three different degrees of resolution, not three competing genres.** Rawlsing can represent a whole year through a few carefully admitted significant developmental episodes, return to a long stretch of mundane cooking, labor and disagreement, and allow one encounter to have a life-altering consequence without assigning any dramatic density to the next. The old SOROK anti-commodification court adds a severe rule: *no event consumes the person who participated in it*. Sex does not make a person “romance content”; an injury does not make them a trauma object; violence does not make the world into a combat game; an emotionally extraordinary encounter does not abolish work, rent, sleep, childcare or inconvenient relatives. That is the most consequential *form* of noncommodification—not a list of respectable event classes but the return of the event to the full ecology of an unfinished life. **Extraordinary intensity can lawfully rearrange priorities, and ordinary obligations continue to have causal force; neither is a product-controlled quota.** The platform must not inject distance to prove independence or intimacy to secure engagement.

**The visitor's unavailable hours are not empty hours for everybody else.** Original SOROK 43 and 46 insist that Nadia's independent calendar continues while the external human has no offscreen autonomous psyche. A third person might cover a shift, a child might learn from another adult, or Nadia might have a perfectly quiet evening. The visitor's diegetic identity can persist without the simulation deciding they helped, apologized, promised, consented or quarreled while absent. Physical nonpresence, deliberate refusal, unread silence, broken commitment, emergency and indifference are not interchangeable facts. **The world continues without authoring either the user's conduct or the user's motives.**

**Absence also reassigns real labor and opportunity, not merely emotional salience.** If the visitor misses a school-pickup commitment, someone else must intervene or a concrete consequence follows. If they simply do not visit for a while, a neighbor may learn the routine, the child's interests may move elsewhere, an open invitation may expire or no relationship change may occur at all. A garden can flourish without the visitor; Nadia may call for her own reasons or not think of them. The original life-progress court demanded that 'no message' and 'nothing notable happened' remain valid results. The UX must neither create melancholy notifications to retain the user nor eliminate the costs of a promise that was actually made. **An ordinary empty return is a legitimate encounter with another life**, not a failed engagement beat.

**Routine is a learned world relation rather than a low-importance filler category.** The first time a visitor cooks in this household, a broad command may require an encounter with actual ingredients, schedules and permission. Months later an established shared method can be carried out without reopening every micro-choice, unless altered circumstances create a genuine fork. Compression thereby marks growing historically situated familiarity without making acquaintance a proficiency meter. The work still consumes time, ingredients and bodily effort. Conversely the character's day cannot be dominated by performative chores merely because the visitor is present to do them; household tasks persist within independent lives rather than appearing as a chain of increasingly moral challenges.

**The life outside the scene is denser than the simulation can enumerate.** The user's November 6 product constitution explicitly settled that Rawlsing is a scientifically guided **artistic and computational hypercompression**: it cannot afford to reenact hundreds of episodes per year, and does not need to claim that it has perfectly reconstructed any actual person's lifetime. Selected developmental episodes may stand for recurring work, accumulated contradiction, prolonged institutional exposure or a concentrated turning point, when bounded historical reasoning supports that representational role. They must not be treated as hundreds of individually verified events, nor multiplied by a universal drama-weight. That problem belongs centrally to SORACLE-CO's model of psychic formation, but it imposes a distinct UX demand: **the visitor must inhabit a life whose practical depth and duration are credible even though most of its past and its ongoing ordinary activity are never simulated at equal resolution**.

The first danger is making the few represented scenes shout “meaningful event!” Every visitor arrival would then be selected as a life-changing crisis, every inquiry as a buried secret, every conversation as a developmental breakthrough. The opposite danger is a mechanically austere fiction with no lived density because the model has only a few explicit episodes to draw upon. The UX synthesis is **unequal resolution without unequal ontological dignity**: use sparse generative histories, plausible recurring practices, institutional conditions, ordinary background continuity and particular current tasks to make an independent life encounterable. A visitor can spend twenty minutes in a kitchen whose history reaches decades without receiving twenty minutes of explanatory flashback. Another event may be individually powerful and historically legitimate. Some moments merely show an existing organization under ordinary pressure, some alter material relations without immediately changing a psyche, and some really do become turning points.

The compression problem therefore is not solved by maximizing event importance. It is solved by **selecting what must become explicit for this historical encounter while preserving the much larger world that those selected parts condition and cannot exhaust**. The visitor may never witness a workday, yet wages, bodily fatigue, a scheduled shift and Nadia's changed availability can be palpable. Recurrent chores do not each require a dramatic vignette; neither does calling them routine erase their social-reproductive labor. A family bond can carry decades of sedimentation that no “meet the brother” scene can adequately summarize. The selected encounter is an opening into density, not a miniature total biography.

**A life continues outside the human's turns, but the experience must not become a second compulsory life to maintain.** Historical continuity can be carried by actual events, recurring practices, world constraints and source-qualified compression; it does not require constant expensive interior narration or a fabricated crisis every time the human is absent. **Re-entering a lived world is not receiving a chapter recap.** The visitor returns at a new situated moment: a chair has moved, a colleague is staying over, someone is working, an item is missing, an ordinary habit has continued. Some changes may be plainly visible; others may become known through a question, a conversation with a different person, a message, a mistaken inference, or never at all. A summary of *administratively resolved continuity* might help a lawful technical recovery, but it cannot be smuggled into the visitor's perception as universal knowledge about what happened while they were away. **The person does not owe a 'here is what I did since your last session' speech.** The visitor may instead find no apparent change, a lost opportunity, a changed relationship, or a new fact whose relevance is obscure. When the user has been absent, their fictional shell must not independently make personal promises, express feelings, undertake meaningful care or explain their silence. Return changes the visitor's access to an already moving day; it does not create that day.

A crucial UX distinction is between **the fictional world's actual calendar** and **the time at which the real human can participate**. An agreed dinner at seven has a diegetic time; its physical scene has a duration determined by actual action. A real human may have a humane wider access window for a scheduled event, without fictional Nadia becoming aware that the user entered the software late. A planned appointment, a soft “market later” opportunity and an immediate “I'm outside now” invitation need not have the same elasticity. An event still waiting for human choice cannot have incompatible downstream consequences precommitted behind their back; when played, the scene must not leak later news backward into the earlier hour. Outside these bounded pending obligations, Nadia and the world proceed.

The other half of this contradiction is **material maintenance without autonomous player agency**. Rent, pay intervals and a preexisting work routine may continue in the thin local shell, while the actual human does not suddenly acquire an independent offscreen personality, new intimate acts, opinions, apologies or promises. One fictional resident continues richly; the visitor remains a persistent identity, resource position and history whose meaningful new action requires the human. The system should never announce to Nadia that the app was closed. It may establish that no reply was sent, that a read receipt was visible if the user actually opened a message, or that a particular agreed meeting did not occur. **Unread silence and read-but-unanswered are different relation facts; a push-notification preview alone is not proof the user read the message.**

“Nadia has an independent life” is insufficient if that life is a queue of impressive offscreen events. What she actually must conduct is an ordinary life across bodily needs, work, relatives, shelter, money, plans, fatigue, institutions, habits, social obligations and desires. Activity in one domain changes what is possible in another. A mother's hospital visit may displace paid work; a late shift affects sleep and dinner; an unexpected visitor can interrupt homework; caring for a friend can make a previously welcomed meeting impossible. These are not interchangeable calendar slots because their meanings, costs and institutional pressures differ.

The same person may possess considerable ability yet have little opportunity to exercise it. The workday consumes time and body; rent creates the necessity of paid labor; a marriage may grant care and also restrict mobility; a social service may protect children while exposing a family to surveillance. A crisis can make one relationship more salient while another continues unnoticed. There is no private psychological miracle that abolishes these conditions. Nor should ordinary activity be reduced to a moral obstacle course arranged for the visitor's development.

Time has several lived and representational grains. A gesture may take a second and invite a lifetime of later interpretation. A dinner can last two hours without transforming anyone. Repeated feeding, transport, schoolwork or small humiliations can acquire durable consequence without a single narratable breakthrough. An offscreen interval may be represented as a qualified recurring practice rather than fabricated detailed conversations. A person's body can grow older while their ideology remains mostly stable, or a framework can change rapidly under an actual rupture while material conditions remain slow and resistant.

The original alpha charter favored the **seduction of ordinary specificity**: the child and the sensitive toilet chain, the kitchen and groceries, a friend's changing arrival time, a cigarette on the landing, a borrowed ingredient, a bus that can be missed. These do not become trivial because they lack a moral revelation. On the contrary, they are the material conditions through which another person's world becomes particular, learnable and resistant to the user's projections.

The independent histories matter even in an apparently identical public world. If Nadia's father's medical circumstances differ after separate branch-world histories, a general health-policy change cannot force the same outcome into both. A father's illness can have one objective progression and several actor-local accounts of what caused it; the sibling's moral or political stance does not prescribe medical reality. Cross-world similarities must be conditional upon actual relevance and exposure, not used to turn institutional policy into a character's predetermined fate.

Public historical events enter by legitimate exposure and cause. A war, election, strike, storm, housing shock or institutional decision may affect Nadia's area and actual relations, perhaps indirectly. The world needs an independently grounded occurrence, actual material consequences and actor-specific channels of knowledge before it can infer whether Nadia has heard or how she reacts. A Palestinian or Lebanese character is not a politics-response template. An actual experience of military power, migration, loss or religious organization may matter deeply while not producing one authorized interpretation. The visitor can know a news event Nadia has never heard about; Nadia can know family consequences the visitor cannot imagine.

The ordinary world can be both deeply historical and remarkably uneventful on a given Thursday. **Independent life is not a drama governor** that forces a crisis whenever a user returns. And a meaningful visit does not displace the other centers of a person's life. It is one activity among several, even when it eventually matters profoundly.

## 7. Door, message, absence: the social form of availability

**A person may be reachable without being available, available without wanting company, and important without being reachable at all.** These are not interface inconveniences. They are historically produced forms of relation. A neighbor's door, a message thread and a prolonged absence distribute space, time, expectation, knowledge and responsibility differently. The visitor can be genuinely interested in knowing Nadia; that interest neither creates an entitlement to her day nor forbids her from wanting the visitor in it. The three conditions of encounter are **co-presence**, **correspondence** and **noncontact**. Each has lawful possibilities and losses that the others cannot simply reproduce. The interface should make this difference inhabitable rather than translate all three into an always-available conversational agent.

**A promise must first become a promise.** An ambiguous 'maybe Friday' is not automatically an accepted seven-o'clock commitment; an actual 'I'll pick up the child at seven' cannot quietly be degraded into casual chat. The older occupancy court required clear temporal legibility before binding an exact appointment, and D002 required attempted speech to pass through lawful event admission. Once admitted, a missed promise can have real costs, but message sending, delivery, actual reading, no reply and failed attendance remain separate histories. A notification preview is not evidence of actual reading; platform telemetry cannot tell Nadia that the human deliberately ignored her. **Responsibility becomes real by making obligations intelligible, not by building fictional omniscience out of device statistics.**

**Co-presence has bodies, places, labor and interruption.** Inside a portal scene the visitor can SAY, DO and ATTEND, subject to their actual local position and the world. A sentence can be interrupted, a gesture misread, a cupboard physically unreachable, a child present without hearing the whole conversation, the bus leaving as the visitor keeps inspecting the refrigerator. The provisional original dual-interface design envisaged a sparse terminal/TI-84-like portal to make the crossing tangible; that aesthetic is an experiment, not the ontology. The ontology is the difference between occupying somebody's space and possessing an always-open text field. A real first scene may end with a meaningful contact route, or with no route at all. The moment of return to the ordinary interface can feel significant without an unlock animation because a finite occasion has actually ended.

**Messaging and correspondence are mediated practical relations, not displaced face-to-face intimacy.** A lawful channel may arise through exchanged numbers, a social group, a shared duty, a third party or a permitted institutional route. It may never arise. With a channel, Nadia can initiate, reply, send a practical question, forward a photo only where source and permission allow, or leave a thread quiet for weeks. She might be at work when a message arrives; another person may interrupt her attempt to write; a shared text joke may become a meaningful practice, or remain a joke. A message sent is not a message delivered; delivery is not opening; opening is not comprehension; comprehension is not acceptance; silence is not a disclosed intention. If a channel has read receipts, their actual negotiated meaning may matter to both participants. A push preview must not quietly become diegetic reading merely because the platform recorded a delivery event. Nadia may infer something from nonresponse, even confidently and wrongly. She may not inherit operating-system metadata as private psychological knowledge.

**Noncontact is itself differentiated by history.** A month without contact may be negligible between casual acquaintances, ordinary in an established friendship, or painful after a missed explicit promise. There is no absence-decay scalar and no duty of daily synthetic maintenance. An actor can reasonably be angry about a missed visit, withdraw after a real broken commitment, become jealous or send a difficult message. That interpersonal difficulty can be genuine, though its source and interpretation remain the CO/world owner's concern. The platform may mediate it; the platform must not select, multiply, prioritize or embellish painful messages because the user has not opened the application. Muting a notification is not a fictional insult, looking at the exit screen is not a confession, and the actual person's departure from Rawlsing is not a diegetic betrayal.

**The outside-portal human retains a stable relational identity but not a secret autonomous psyche.** The fictional shell can have previously admitted lodging, work hours, material costs, routine sleep or basic maintenance across intervals in which the real human is absent. These do not authorize the platform to make a new promise, recite invented autobiography, cultivate another romance, apologize, consent, argue politics or resolve a conflict on the human's behalf. The initial bounded fictional prelude has a different status: voluntarily entering Rawlsing can license a mundane premise that makes the first encounter coherent, provided it cannot author significant later choices. Outside active participation, an invitation can arrive and go unanswered; the other person and the world may solve the practical situation without forging the human's yes or no. Background material continuity is not personal autopilot.

**A deeper contradiction emerges between the historical calendar of an independent life and the external human's finite ability to appear.** Original SOROK 43 and 46 pressed present-coupled life and real missed chances; 47 and 48 proposed *elastic rendezvous* as the anti-coercive mediation. Under one provisional form, Nadia's Friday-seven dinner has one diegetic time while the user has a bounded real-world opportunity to play it later. If they attend within that window, they should not be diegetically late solely because the app opened later; after resolution the world can catch up. If the window expires, a missed commitment may become history. Close-synchronization or stronger real-time play may be offered only by explicit, informed opt-in, reversible prospectively and never a hidden condition of affection, permission, relational warrant or prestige. How broad each event window should be remains an experimental, not philosophical, conclusion.

**But elasticity is not a free suspension of other people.** The proposed pending Friday encounter creates a world-history problem: Nadia's friend may already have needed an answer, a child may fall ill, a work shift may change, or some later event may depend on whether the dinner happened. If the simulation resolves Saturday history before the human enters the pending Friday scene, it cannot merely paste the later visit into a world whose causes have already been settled. If it freezes all other actors to await the visitor, it denies the independence that justified elasticity. The conceptual limit is a bounded, explicit causal dependency: what truly depends on Friday must be held, conditionally qualified or coherently re-resolved by the competent world owner; what does not depend on Friday may continue. Later evidence must not leak backward into the Friday phenomenal surface. This is a **held architecture problem**, not a newly issued scheduler, a second world or an assertion that the full conditional mechanism already runs.

**Temporal flexibility must be symmetrical in respect without pretending to be symmetrical in control.** A human can decline or counteroffer an exact appointment; Nadia may agree, object, plan around it or refuse. An ephemeral opportunity—'I'm downstairs now'—may legitimately expire more quickly than an arranged meal. The interface can make an actual diegetic commitment intelligible before the visitor accepts it, without disguising the real user's calendar burden. The visitor should be able to choose nonparticipation while recognizing that an already promised undertaking has an afterlife. A service that conceals its own scheduling concessions from Nadia should not pretend Nadia personally extended grace; a service that hides its restrictions from the human should not then diagnose unreliability.

**Scene endings are social events, not chapters in a funnel.** Nadia has a shift, another person arrives, an argument consumes the occasion, or the conversation simply runs out. If a contact channel has actually been established, **whether Nadia initiates another contact must have an actor-and-world reason**: a practical question she cannot easily answer alone, a remembered remark that now matters to her, an obligation, amusement, anxiety, a mistaken expectation, a fresh desire to talk, or a message she sends out of habit. No rule requires an outgoing message as the visitor's reward for ending a scene or as the platform's response to absence. Her other interests can interrupt an intended message before it is sent. She may begin composing and choose not to send; may send one line before work and fall silent; may ask a question then decide she has no time for the answer. A message is itself an outward act with a recipient, time and possible third-party awareness—not a chatbot automatically awakening when the real human opens an app. **No contact can be as socially intelligible as contact** without becoming a secret refusal penalty. On return, a message thread may carry a particular unfinished history or be entirely quiet. A relation can become significant through letters, appointments, chance meetings or sparse correspondence, and its medium can change without changing the underlying person's identity.

The old theological and materialist wager acquires an exact UX form here: **being answerable to another life does not mean being continuously summonable by a platform**. One can make a promise that matters and nevertheless refuse permanent capture. Another can genuinely want us and nevertheless possess a day that does not revolve around our access. The humane interface is neither a frictionless companion nor a punishment machine. It is a limited passage among real possibilities of presence, communication and absence, through which both generosity and harm can have histories.

## 8. You become known and misrecognized as you try to know

**The reciprocal gaze becomes more serious when the system also admits that its own model of the visitor is a fourth, fallible point of view.** The human understands Nadia through particular speech, acts and absences. Nadia develops a model of the human through **only** the outward conduct she can actually perceive and through her historical formation under SORACLE-CO. Rawlsing may record narrower evidence that the visitor repeatedly chooses certain objects, signals expertise or answers difficult questions. These three representations can diverge even when each is locally useful. The system's statistical inference does not become the visitor's essence; Nadia's confident impression does not become the system's certainty; the human's self-interpretation does not become a guaranteed source of their actual outward performance. **NO PARTICIPANT IS EXHAUSTED BY ANOTHER PARTICIPANT'S MODEL OF THEM—INCLUDING THE REAL HUMAN.**

**The more completely an actor believes the visitor is 'one of us,' the more their justified expectations may diverge from the visitor's actual formation.** A locally accepted neighbor may be trusted to know a school routine, read a religious practice or recognize a status insult that an imported consciousness does not understand. The resulting errors can affect someone else's labor or access; they are not proof that a single local culture has defeated the visitor. Nor can the system's foreknowledge that the human is an external participant become Nadia's knowledge or an explanation of her reaction. **The visitor may be socially legible without being historically equivalent, and socially misunderstood while nevertheless learning a genuine practice through participation.**

**Each observer's theory of the relationship can be wrong while an objectively shared obligation is real.** If Nadia thinks a co-parenting routine signifies reconciliation, the visitor thinks it is purely practical, and an older child treats it as a reliable part of life, the same repeated acts sustain three histories of expectation. Someone's failure to show up can materially harm the child even if the adult sincerely denies the intimacy another inferred. Neither actor's private interpretation governs the other's memory, and no disembodied relationship score adjudicates their mutual meaning. This is a more demanding account of reciprocal misrecognition: people can cooperate successfully, become interdependent, and retain fundamental disagreement about what they are together.

**Being seen, being approved of and being understood are three different relational possibilities.** The visitor can legitimately desire all three, just as Nadia can. A clever anecdote, a thoughtful question, a show of competence, an apology or an act of practical help may elicit a confident but mistaken judgment from Nadia, or may be ignored for reasons outside the visitor's awareness. Repeated questions about her opinion of the visitor are actual conduct; they cannot by themselves authorize an inferred attachment diagnosis, stable moral character or hidden motive. The visitor's own interpretation can become causally effective without becoming accurate. Likewise, Nadia's assessment may organize her future acts while remaining false. Court 11's distinction between realized praxis, voluntary self-report and uncertain system hypothesis thereby becomes part of what the encounter can be: the human has a history within the game but is never exhausted by the history the platform chooses to tell about them.

Consider a visitor who uses a sophisticated literary reference to reassure Nadia. The phrase may work because the visitor knows its social significance, not its deeper historical content. Nadia might be impressed, irritated, skeptical, indifferent or led to ask an advanced question. The user may later demonstrate real sustained competence, strategically bluff, or be exposed through an accidental remark. None of those cases permits the platform to classify their true education or external social class. Nor may Nadia be made aware of the platform's private estimate. **The social performance itself is a legitimate human action**, with possible benefits, misrecognitions, expectations and embarrassments. The visitor's power to make a truthful or deceptive impression is distinct from their power to control what the Other concludes.

**Reciprocity is also the visitor's encounter with their own non-sovereignty.** It is one thing to know intellectually that other minds exist. It is another to experience how a word one meant kindly is received as intrusive, how a justified criticism is heard as betrayal, or how an act undertaken to help becomes material for a stranger's independent judgment. The visitor can discover that another person's interpretation has causes, that their own initial account may have been incomplete, and that neither person's immediate experience abolishes what actually occurred. Yet this does not prescribe deference: Nadia's judgment can be unjust, culturally conventional, self-protective or simply mistaken. **Formation through the Other is not obedience to the Other.** The generative experience is the unresolved task of being answerable to objective consequences while retaining a real capacity to disagree, set boundaries and criticize domination.

**Reciprocity is mediated by the Other's senses, not conferred by co-presence.** The November 9 SOROK sensory-access conception distinguished three separate links: *the visitor's actual committed outward conduct* (entering, stopping, speaking, turning, leaving), *Nadia's lawful partial sensory opportunity* (hearing, peripheral sight, facing, obstruction, competing work and exact temporal overlap), and *Nadia's actor-local interpretation and chosen response*. A human glances privately at something, but their private orientation does not automatically become a readable signal in Nadia's mind. A physical head turn may become an actual visible cue; her suspicion about its meaning can still be false. If Nadia is facing the stove with a pot boiling, she may hear steps without seeing the target of the visitor's gaze; she may be distracted enough not to register the steps, or may notice and do nothing.

**The same event can become many historically different experiences without becoming many different events.** The original interaction system and CAN-008 R2 distinguish a fixed admitted occurrence; what each actor could physically sense; what each actually registered; uptake and appraisal through that person's bodily needs, previous relationships, learned meanings, memories and current task; recruitment of one or more competing possible responses; what they themselves can consciously access; what they outwardly do or inhibit; and which consequences are sufficiently grounded to enter later person/relationship/history owners. Nadia may be busy enough not to hear a joke her child hears plainly. Both may hear the same words and understand them, yet find different concerns recruited. Two acquaintances might find the same offer generous or intrusive because their histories with the visitor differ. The *same person* might receive the same words differently from a sibling, stranger, lover or supervisor; their relation-indexed history is causal, not a label that selects canned dialogue. **Distinct processing does not require distinct visible behavior**: two people can both continue washing dishes despite different private effects. Conversely similar expression need not imply similar feelings, and some encounters leave no durable psychological change. The observer sees only the resulting lawful outer situation, not the invisible multi-stream derivation. The UX must conserve one actual event across all recipients and refuse a display model's temptation to invent a more colorful reaction. This is an inherited person×event function to consume and test against actual owners, **not** a proposed NEIGHBOR second uptake engine.

**A response is not a mandatory receipt for a successful input.** Original D002's fifty hostile scenarios show a very particular UX danger: a story agent often feels obliged to produce a bespoke Nadia reply to every visitor message. Yet she may hear a remark while tending a boiling pot and a child and simply continue; half-hear; understand everything but choose one practical thread; defer a response; or not find the remark salient enough for durable memory. Silence does not automatically mean incomprehension, psychological defense, disapproval or consent. A long user message about Rome, homework and a relationship concern may receive a one-line reply about tomorrow's schedule even though each part was comprehended. The unfinished social situation remains actual.

**What is hidden and what is expressed are produced by a person, not chosen to maximize mystery.** An exhausted Nadia can answer sideways; a playful Nadia may interrupt; a guarded Nadia might withhold an account. Her actual acquired expressive repertoire may also make private currents bodily visible: a spontaneous laugh, familiar gesture, change in breathing, unguarded warmth, flushed face, breaking voice or failed concealment. Such events are permissible only when they arise from her history, body, present multi-stream situation and actual expressive functioning and are lawfully admitted as outward occurrences. The opposite outcome—successful suppression, ordinary unmarked speech, no tell, or a deliberate staged signal—is equally legitimate. **Opacity is not a mandate to hide genuine expression, and legibility is not permission to manufacture a clue.** A visible tremor may really occur without revealing its cause; a familiar look may be correctly read without forcing Nadia to congratulate the visitor. Different viewers may register the same gesture differently under real observation conditions. The renderer must neither convert every private activation into a revealing eyebrow movement nor rewrite a real expressive event into opacity merely to preserve the idea that people are inexhaustible. It must also resist the generic assistant's impulse to answer every question comprehensively or randomly insert 'humanizing' quirks. The expressive possibility belongs to the actor/CO owner, world occurrence to the event owner, finite recognition to NEIGHBOR's presentation, and the visitor's causal interpretation to the visitor unless legitimately expressed.

This is one of the deepest social experiences in the game: the visitor can be intensely interested in a moment that mattered almost nothing to the Other, or regard a joke as minor when a child remembers it for years. Several recipients can hear the same words and become differently affected. Nadia's relative silence is not the world's only consequence. The actual encounter is neither a response-generator nor a bilateral scoreboard; it is a participation in a shared environment where no one guarantees a reply.

**The reality of the relation is not exhausted by either person's feeling for the other, or by their two models considered side by side.** Two people can learn a greeting, recurring joke, mutual pause, pattern of silence, practical rhythm or way of asking for help that did not exist in either life before they met. The repeated practice is objectively historical; it can become easier to enact and recognizable to third parties. But its psychic afterlives are **two histories before they are any common meaning**. The visitor may experience a Tuesday call as cherished intimacy; Nadia may find it useful, comforting, burdensome and genuinely affectionate while refusing the exclusivity the visitor assumes. The visitor's pleasure may depend upon being specifically recognized; Nadia's pleasure may depend upon no longer having to perform an elaborate introduction. Each can anticipate the other's interpretation and be wrong, and their mistaken expectations can become a further causal force. **The 'between' is therefore not a third knowing subject or relationship-soul. It is the real, jointly produced field of events, learned conventions, obligations, bodily co-activity, unequal stakes, counterpart-specific psychic orientations, mutually fallible representations and network consequences that neither person owns.** Its shared practices can be accurate without its participants having matching inner experiences; genuine disagreement can persist underneath a fluent routine.

**A dyad can acquire libidinal or cathectic gravity without acquiring one joint appetite.** In this theoretical usage, cathexis names *historically constituted investment*—wanting this person's notice, enjoying a particular activity with them, anticipating criticism, hoping to be invited, fearing loss, resenting obligation, competing for recognition, desiring intimacy, feeling relief in their presence, or worrying about one's own desire. Some of these may reinforce one another; others inhibit, displace or transform each other. The same gesture from Nadia can recruit in the visitor a pleasure of recognition, a suspicion of condescension and the impulse to reciprocate; Nadia's own currents may be altogether different. Their prior relationship has made these recruitments possible and sometimes probable, but no global attraction, attachment, trust, libido or 'chemistry' score may determine the result. **The sharp contradiction is that the same shared history can produce more fluency and more uneven vulnerability simultaneously**: a joke can become easier to make even as it comes to mean something painful to one participant. Nor does one participant's interpretation of the other's activated currents become fact about that Other.

That makes a productive UX capacity possible: **a person can become better at participating in a shared practice while becoming less confident that they know the other's inner valuation**. The routine becomes easier to conduct, and the relation more intricate rather than simpler. An apparent repair may restore the ordinary greeting without repairing the underlying injury. A rupture can make one familiar gesture painful to one person and ordinary to the other. Over-reading, under-reading, asymmetric transitions, indirect negotiation and persistent ambiguity are not noise to clean out of a dyad. They are among the phenomena NEIGHBOR-UX is here to make experientially legible.

**The original beginning-to-know movement is reciprocal, but reciprocity is not a requirement for equal numbers of questions.** The visitor notices something, chooses what to disclose or ask, and makes a tentative interpretation. Meanwhile the visitor is becoming a particular person *in Nadia's experience*. What makes her ask **this** question of **this** human **now**? Perhaps the visitor uses an unusual word, demonstrates an ability she needs, shares a memory she cannot fit into her local categories, offers help at a cost, consistently misreads a cue, or simply becomes pleasant company while she has time. Her interest can be practical, affectionate, wary, status-seeking, flirtatious, irritated, merely conversational, or not formed at all; several such orientations can conflict. A question can be spontaneous, withheld, rehearsed, badly timed, aborted, or asked for a reason she herself misunderstands. **Her question is also an action upon the visitor.** It can expose her expectation, invite disclosure, demand proof, corner someone into politeness, offer intimacy or give them a chance to refuse. The human may answer truthfully, joke, decline, lie or fail to find a local translation for their actual experience. Neither participant owns the other's response, and the platform may not turn the exchange into a symmetrical interview bot or a sequence of personality facts unlocked by curiosity.

The first success of a relationship may be almost imperceptible as a game achievement and unmistakable as a human event: Nadia asks without being prompted; an unfinished joke returns; the visitor recognizes when to stop asking; a name is remembered; Nadia asks for the visitor's opinion; the conversation continues after the practical reason is gone; next time she seems guarded because of something previously said. These are **changed reciprocal expectations**, not a hidden intimacy rank. An early failure may be a short answer, missed joke, ordinary impatience or a conversation that dies because the visitor monopolizes it. One awkward minute is not a final verdict. Real curiosity can be mutual, one-sided or absent.

The crucial UX complication is that **self-presentation is not self-verification**. A human can truthfully disclose a life, make a claim the product cannot independently verify, exaggerate or knowingly invent a spectacular identity. The product cannot call them a liar merely because their claim sounds implausible, nor infer verified real-world ability from a text declaration. It can distinguish a user assertion, an admitted local capability, a capability actually exercised through the medium, and an independently established material fact. Nadia hears only what she actually hears and may believe, doubt, laugh, admire, be irritated or find the claim irrelevant. The visitor's narrated virtuosity is not the same as the visitor's real explanation, memory, humor or practical reasoning performed through the interface.

This reconstitutes the moral tension: **honesty need not be enforced to be existentially richer**. A person who claims they can play a Chopin nocturne, recite an Arabic surah and do two hundred push-ups may generate a local reputation and later obligations if such abilities were admitted for the run. Nadia may go on cooking, be amused, ask for help teaching music, find it pretentious or depart for work halfway through. The spectacle cannot summon an applauding audience or force love. A user who simply learns the child's name, asks a real question and returns to an unfinished task may enter more reciprocal history without receiving a truthfulness bonus. The world can support both choices and let their different demands, costs and afterlives accumulate.

The bill-glance case crystallizes the mutual-gaze theory, but it becomes fully constitutive only when placed beside the historical self-myth theory. Nadia can believe the visitor read a number they never saw. The visitor can think Nadia caught them when she was thinking about something else. Both beliefs may recruit prior shame, vigilance, household experience, curiosity or desire to appear generous. Their next speech may intensify or dissipate the misunderstanding without anyone learning the truth.

The world has a real prior bill, a price, a location and causal effects on the payer. It does not need to identify one “true social meaning” of the glance. The user's head movement and any cover speech are objective occurrences; their recognition of the text, Nadia's attention to the movement, each party's beliefs about what the other knows, and later interpretations are different. A false belief can be a real cause. If Nadia starts hiding papers, her subjective inference has generated actual world history without having become factually correct. If the user apologizes for something she never noticed, the apology can create suspicion for the first time.

This is not an invitation to generate infinite nested mind models. A particular belief about another person's belief matters if it becomes causally relevant in this history. Other beliefs can remain unknown, inactive or transitory. A third person may interpret the apology differently or later learn about the bill without having been present. The relation exceeds either participant's account because its consequences are distributed through several bodies, activities, memories and institutions, not because the dyad has a unified hidden soul.

The older framework-reinterpretation documents add temporal depth. Nadia may later tell herself she has always been too trusting, reinterpret the bill encounter as warning, or disregard it. If she acquires a defensive view of the visitor, contradictory later generosity may be assimilated as manipulation. Or a genuine material act of help may challenge the framework. Neither the truth of the action nor the actor's interpretation guarantees a revised relation. An interpretation can become self-confirming. A later more generous reading can also omit relevant harm.

The same applies to the real visitor. Their account of Nadia can become progressively more differentiated while remaining mistaken in particular places. The system should not present the user's attention history as a clinical personality diagnosis. A very perceptive visitor may still fail to notice how their interpretive framework selects evidence; another may be rude yet accidentally correct about a practical fact. Successful learning is not the game's automated moral status.

The resulting principle is more exact than “no mind-reading”: **no actor may obtain another's experience by borrowing the system's explanation, and no actor's false experience may rewrite objective history merely by becoming powerful**. Both world causality and the causality of mistaken human understanding remain real.

## 9. Access is changing reachability, not a relationship level

**Access is an arrangement of social powers, not a measure of whether the visitor is liked.** A household door may be open because the resident invited them, because a neighbor has the key, because the building's material conditions allow it, or because an employer or landlord imposes a different right. Physical entry, permission to look, permission to borrow, permission to attend a gathering and entitlement to demand private explanation are different. Class, family authority, employment, disability, age, religion, rent and legally enforceable institutions can make a theoretically friendly relationship materially inaccessible—or allow material access while affection is entirely absent. **An authorized route can exist without a live occasion, and a lived occasion can yield no standing permission.** This is where the sociology of property, reproduction and inherited institutions becomes a felt UX rather than expository world-building.

**Trust, recognition and duty also remain domain-specific.** Nadia can expect the visitor to arrive punctually at a school gate and still mistrust them with money, believe they are safe around her child yet avoid ideological arguments with them, or trust a former friend in a technical task while refusing personal disclosure. Both might say 'we are friends' and mean different things; a third party might treat them as family without either agreeing. One injury may be repaired in its practical consequences without restoring the old willingness to be vulnerable; one sustained act of care may change a future role without increasing affection. These distinctions are not imperfections of a trust score—they are the real social plurality a scalar would erase.

**An introduction is a change in the conditions of possible participation, not a transferred claim over a third party.** A caregiver may authorize one pickup from school without giving the visitor general access to a child; Nadia may allow a visitor into her kitchen but not her neighbor's apartment; a workplace may admit a guest to a lobby without licensing a conversation with an employee. These limitations are not reducible to how strongly Nadia feels. An unwelcome but legally permitted person can obtain access through an institution; a dearly loved person can be excluded by a building's employment or security regime. Thus every apparently relational route carries a political history of property, authority and social reproduction. The original Q-ACCESS-001 sequence—route, eligibility, occasion, user attempt, realized contact—must not be silently compressed to 'Nadia granted access.'

**Relational access is reachability across a changing social topology, not ascent along an access ladder.** Original SOROK Q-ACCESS-001 was considerably more exact than the current HOME: a person's world includes places, rooms, communication channels, projects, workplaces, institutions, obligations, people, events and resources, each of which may be reachable through a distinct route. A route has a *target*, a *mode* of access, a *basis* (invitation, public right, standing permission, joint practice, possession, emergency), a *controller*, limited *scope*, *conditions*, *duration*, *revocability*, relevant *dependencies* and an actual *historical origin*. These are distinctions in the experience of what the human can do, not instructions to create a second canonical relationship psyche.

**Existence of a route is not permission, permission is not a current occasion, a current occasion is not an attempt, and an attempt is not realized presence.** Having Nadia's contact does not make her available to respond. Being regularly invited to the kitchen does not entitle the visitor to enter the bedroom, inspect private papers, attend a school event or borrow a key. A particular invitation may expire. Third parties, children, workplaces, landlords and public institutions can control access Nadia herself cannot grant. Even *being physically present* does not mean Nadia disclosed anything, that the user noticed what was there, or that the visitor acquired truthful knowledge.

Access can expand, narrow, become conditional, lapse or migrate across domains. A rupture can close the household door while an occasional messaging channel remains. A new job removes one old route. Greater intimacy can make some private boundaries sharper, not weaker. A practical family routine may end because it has ceased to be needed, without any loss of affection. The visitor may become close while never meeting Nadia's mother; they may be permitted to pick up the child on one occasion without being trusted with the family finances. This is not “relationship progression” hidden behind a more sophisticated multigraph; it is **the material geography of changing participation**.

The conceptual payoff is multiplicative. NEIGHBOR's world starts to feel larger precisely when the user's access becomes more particular. As they learn an actual route to a friend's work, a sibling's gathering or a shared neighborhood activity, they encounter people whose own permissions and agendas interrupt the fantasy of Nadia as the sole gateway to all meaningful life. **A relationship becomes more real by changing the world of possible co-activity, not by completing the Other's content.**

The original provisional relational ontology offered an indispensable distinction: person-stable organization is not relation-free, but the person is not exhausted by any dyad. A relation may be made of shared actual activities, remembered promises, access, practical reliance, unequal resources, both people's representations, third-party expectations and institutional rights. The relation can exceed what either partner knows of it without possessing a sovereign mind or a universal “relationship state” that decides how both must behave.

Mother, Father, Nadia, a brother and a visiting neighbor do not form a star of separately scored links pointing at Nadia. Nadia's understanding of Father is changed by her model of how Father treats Mother. She may think Mother's criticisms are unfair, later learn of infidelity, and reread the exact same earlier wage work and family meals as part of a different story. The earlier labor happened. The later betrayal happened. Her new interpretation can be partly right and still unjust to some of the old history. Her brother may interpret the same relations through a learned sense that he must protect Mother; that protection can constrain Nadia even when he sincerely loves her. Their mother can value both children and be changed by their conflict while having her own interests, work and unexpressed doubts.

**A social scene is produced and reproduced through relations among relations, not through the visitor's serial interviews with independent NPCs.** Nadia and her sister may be finishing a conversation begun hours earlier. Their father may be materially absent but anticipated as a sanction; a child may be listening for an entirely different reason; a friend may enter with a practical demand that alters who can stay. The visitor encounters an existing *collective activity* with its own subject matter, unevenly shared memories, alliances, humor, claims to speak, responsibilities, bodily positions and constraints. No narrator may silently convert their conversation into expository lines addressed to the visitor. The visitor may be welcomed into the exchange, spoken around, deliberately excluded, misunderstood, ignored, protected or asked to leave. **Others can address each other while the visitor listens; one Other can interrupt another; a third may correct a story that Nadia sincerely believes; a person may change how she speaks because of this particular audience.** The truth of the event, each actor's accessible part of it and each participant's privately recruited stakes remain separate. Together they create new social conditions that no one participant has selected. The point is not a multiplayer mode or community-organizing objective; it is that being Nadia's neighbor includes being present in the social relations through which her life already moves.

**The conversational floor is itself materially and relationally contested.** A parent, employer, child, charismatic friend, guest or exhausted worker does not have the same practical entitlement or opportunity to interrupt. A silence can be shared ease, polite deference, a child's exclusion, fear of sanction, absorbed work or simple lack of interest; it must not be converted into one universal sign of consent or intimacy. A visitor's arrival can make Nadia more talkative with her sister, less candid, protective of the visitor, annoyed at the disruption, or entirely unchanged. Their presence has actual possible consequences; it does not confer ownership of everyone else's attention. **Polycentric social life means not just several psychological recipients of one event, but several people making events with and against one another while differently understanding what they are doing.** The common activity can endure as a household convention or shared project without becoming a household supermind.

A particular historically situated possibility from the original NEIGHBORMULCH conversation makes the field concrete. Imagine adolescent Nadia in southern Lebanon, drawn to certain Western songs, books and a developing ideal of freedom while simultaneously loyal to family practices and uncertain about her own atheism. Her father works or lives abroad with a new household; his material absence does not make him causally absent. Her brother becomes strongly identified with a masculine responsibility toward their mother. Nadia and her brother can be profoundly loving and intimate siblings while his sincere protective identity generates restrictions she resists. Their mother possesses her own labor, affections, health, grief and judgments, which cannot simply be explained as the consequence of Nadia's disobedience. The family's transnational conditions, religious and gendered norms, obligations and particular biographies jointly matter. This is a **bounded hypothetical**, not a claim about all Lebanese families or an automatic identity-to-psychology rule. The original user passage associated with this example ended with an unfinished “and the”; its ending must remain unconstructed until directly supplied.

The user can become part of these configurations without becoming their center. Helping Nadia's child with homework may genuinely assist the child, complicate Nadia's authority, alter a third adult's plans, or leave no durable mark on Nadia. A new practice learned through the visitor may later be exercised independently. A strong attachment may form that remains important without creating a superior claim over Nadia's other commitments. Someone may love the visitor and deliberately reduce contact because work or family demands it, with neither the love nor the decision being discredited.

The conceptual fusion also creates a limit: more relational data are not automatically better anthropology. A graph of every person's opinion about everyone else would still omit bodily life, implicit practices, institutions, and unarticulated history. A person can also develop in solitude, loss, absence, fear of imagined judgment, memory and anticipation. Relational constitution does not require continual physical company. Independence is constituted through actual dependency, opportunities to separate, mastered practices and changing relations; it is not the absence of relations.

## 10. The relationship becomes specific without a progress score

**The sharpest normative counterexample is affection that coexists with real domination.** A person may love, need and defend the visitor while losing access to friends, resources, alternative care or the freedom to refuse. The visitor may feel indispensable and call that love. A different relationship may provoke anger through a necessary refusal, maintain outside ties, increase practical agency or create a dignified boundary without improving the visitor's popularity. **Attachment, gratitude, dependence, compliance, sexual intimacy, material benefit, self-trust, capacity to exit, social belonging and flourishing are partly incommensurable histories**, not slices of one approval curve.

**A stable routine can acquire contradictory meanings without falling apart.** The early SOROK differential-relational court treated objective convention, actor A's valuation, actor B's valuation, actor A's model of B and actor B's model of A as separate. A weekly shared meal may give both participants welcome food and reliable coordination while one interprets it as a friendship becoming familial and the other as practical neighborly exchange. The recurrence can produce real expectations and even care without requiring interpretive convergence. One participant might suspect a breach while the other thinks nothing changed; the visitor may be right that something coercive occurred while Nadia sincerely experiences it as ordinary. Neither the platform's relationship label nor a private psychic reading may erase the common practice or certify one interpretation as the only meaning. This is why historical relationship formation is not the monotonic accumulation of an agreed social score.

The old D003 and Court 10 collision prevents two easy answers. The game cannot quietly define a single modern liberal-autonomous individual as healthy, thereby pathologizing caregiving, communal obligation, material dependency, religious self-surrender or grief. But it also cannot call a relationship flourishing solely because a local norm approves, the person insists on happiness, or the platform is afraid to make a normative distinction. **Local norm, the person's valuation, practical functioning, actual relational effect and a contestable normative appraisal are separate claims.** A migrant sending wages home may lose discretionary money and gain meaningful belonging; a family duty may sustain life and reproduce coercion at once; a grieving person may depend intensely for months without showing pathological decline. A change can be domain-specific, temporally ambiguous, and only partly attributable to the visitor. D003 remains an **open P0 court**, not a hidden expert health evaluator adopted by this HOME.

**The founding distinction among attachment, trust, health, pleasure, compliance, intimacy, benefit, dependency and access is a political-psychological one.** Different practices can make each grow or shrink independently. A visitor might become Nadia's favorite companion while narrowing her other ties; might be trusted with a child while lacking intimacy with the mother; might help materially while being disliked; might refuse a request and cause immediate pain while protecting another obligation. A hidden “good relationship” meter would translate those concrete contradictions into one false social value. Conversely, having no meter is not a declaration that every consequence is morally equal. Harm, coercion and freedom have material histories even when participants cannot interpret them accurately.

**The visitor's formation is similarly plural.** A human may become more skillful at making apologies while more dependent on receiving forgiveness; more sensitive to cultural difference while still overlooking labor; better at recognizing coercion while using that knowledge to assert control. No simple humility or compassion score can capture this. Longitudinal reflection may eventually ask what they repeatedly *did*, across which constraints and with what alternatives, allowing counterexamples and uncertainty. Its purpose is to give the visitor something real to think with, **not to announce what kind of soul the platform has discovered**.

**A relationship can become experientially richer through pleasure and acquired fluency without yielding a higher relationship score or a deeper secret.** The particular persons discover that they enjoy some of the same company, teasing, games, silences, musical practices, walks, arguments or ordinary work. A joke acquires a history and can later be funny without needing to be explained. Nadia anticipates that the visitor will understand a reference; the visitor learns when their company is welcome, when a silence is genuinely easy, or how to pass a tool without asking. Repeated co-activity may give them a *jointly learned practical ability*: making something, singing together, solving an ordinary problem, inhabiting a familiar rhythm that neither could perform in quite that form before they met. **This is positive historical becoming, not the absence of conflict.** Neither pleasure nor the skill of participating proves they know one another's causes, agree about what the relation means, or should continue it. A fluent practice can conceal an unequal labor burden, an unspoken expectation or a growing injury. A rough, difficult meeting can still be profoundly wanted. A friendship can become warmer while a particular boundary sharpens; a pattern can become effortless for one and increasingly exhausting for the other. The visible changes are concrete acts, changed expectations, better-timed or mistimed participation, actual initiative, withdrawal and different kinds of continuing possibility—not points, rewards or compulsory intimacy.

**The pleasure of companionship has no entitlement to reproduce itself.** A person might enjoy seeing another today, desire them tomorrow, dread an obligation on Friday, and later return to an old affectionate rhythm without having 'lost' or 'regained' a fixed quantity of closeness. Laughter can be playful, nervous, defensive, exclusionary, tender or simply funny. Comfort can permit candid disagreement; it can also make certain differences harder to mention. A pair may develop a private comic shorthand that a third person cannot understand, while that third person reasonably experiences the same exchange as exclusion. **Shared ease is a real accomplishment of history; it is not self-certifying evidence of shared understanding, moral health or equal enjoyment.** This is precisely why it can remain attractive as lived experience without becoming a designed retention inducement.

The original SOROK source constitution is radical precisely because it refuses to equate the visitor's reward with Nadia's good. Benefit can occur without recognition. Injury can occur without detection. Actual flourishing need not produce approval, attachment or visible ease. Conversely, someone's strong attachment may be a real source of purpose and help, not a moral defect that a product should counterbalance with artificially manufactured independence.

The Constitutive Organism's whole-person challenge makes this sharper. Nadia can have a profound experience with the visitor that changes a private practice the visitor never witnesses. She can develop a relationship with a third party because of something they did together. She can acquire competence she later exercises independently. She can remain unchanged by something the visitor regarded as life-altering. Or she can genuinely love the visitor, depend upon them, build a life with them and make the relation one of her deepest commitments. Neither the engine nor a hidden moral governor may privilege the first set as proof of otherness or the second as a completion reward.

**There is no computationally preferred outcome of increasing intimacy, decreasing intimacy, independence, dependency, forgiveness or estrangement.** Those are historically possible relational states and acts, not concealed goals of the product. The user can be careful, exploitative, generous, sexually desirous, angry, courageous or mistaken. Nadia can reciprocate, refuse, manipulate, withdraw, love, injure, misunderstand or become controlling. An independent Other does not mean a sanitized Other. The environment may include adult sexuality, unconventional consensual arrangements, conflict, theft, work humiliation, intense intimacy and unwanted coercion where lawful and genuinely caused. If a fictional adult undertakes paid sexual labor, that choice belongs to a materially specific configuration of wages, coercion, law, agency, risk, pleasure, dependence and social relations; it does **not** automatically establish a particular childhood trauma, a punitive arc or an eventual conversion to the narrator's idea of respectability. Distinguish sex work as actual labor under unequal institutions from a visitor's entitlement to another person's sexual availability. Neither idealize nor universally pathologize the person. The presence of such events does not require the program to organize them as content funnels or produce a universal therapeutic interpretation.

Consent to voluntary joint activity must be independently authored by the participating people. This does not imply that unilateral harm, coercion or institutional violence require victim permission; people can be harmed without consenting. Conversely, the user cannot write another person's desire or consent into existence by declaring it. A false accusation can harm a sibling; a secret theft can deplete resources even if no one knows the visitor did it; a later confession can alter attribution without rewriting the theft. Anonymous help can improve a child's opportunities while giving the user no closeness reward.

The original “kenotic attention” hypothesis acquires more precise meaning under this world. It is not self-erasure, local-norm obedience or a command to accept Nadia's account. It means relinquishing the sovereignty of the user's present model of her without surrendering agency or moral judgment. A person can attend deeply and still resist coercion, leave a harmful relation, refuse a request, or protect another vulnerable person. The system must allow the user to say “No, you are wrong,” without treating it as failure to care.

Nor is care reducible to moral victory through persuasion. The visitor may be right and powerless, wrong and influential, kind but ineffective, or helpful without fully understanding what they helped. A particular relationship can become harder as its participants learn more about one another. The reward for genuine intimacy is **more reality**, not necessarily easier access.

The original source's hardest counterexample is the **visitor whose selfishness works**. Someone may manipulate, intimidate or exploit Nadia and obtain what they sought, at least for a time. The world does not owe an immediate punishment, a corrective character speech or a reliable inverse relationship score. Another person's genuine dependence or mistake can make domination effective. Material and relational harm remain real even if nobody recognizes it as harm, and the visitor cannot certify its nonexistence by feeling successful. A theory of encounter that secretly makes every harmful choice backfire has become an ethical teaching game that manufactures consequences instead of representing them. That failure is different from platform policy preventing genuinely unsafe or nonconsensual representation; normative product boundaries and fictional world causation must stay distinct.

The game has an ethical horizon because actual human agency meets another irreducible center. That horizon does not become a measurement system. The service may not legislate a single good life for Nadia or assign a moral mark to the visitor. It can preserve objective harm, institutional coercion and practical consequences without telling the human what self-congratulatory moral to extract.

## 11. A social world makes another encounter possible without awarding access

The visitor enters one person's particular life rather than selecting from a collection of unlockable characters. **As the relationship develops, other people can become encounterable because they already exist in that person's working, familial, neighborhood and institutional world.** A friend arrives, a cousin needs a ride, a coworker speaks, or the visitor independently crosses a social route. Introduction is neither an achievement badge nor an automatic transfer of Nadia's influence over somebody else.

**Every encounter has its own authority.** Nadia may invite the visitor to dinner; her sister may decline to talk. Nadia may provide a phone number with permission, without giving permanent access to its owner. The visitor may misread an introduction as intimacy or mistake an ordinary courtesy for membership. Another person may be interested in the visitor for reasons Nadia could not foresee. The user can also meet someone whom Nadia neither knows nor likes; being Nadia's neighbor does not make her the owner of the neighborhood.

**Shared precontact origins do not create shared later personhood.** Separate real humans may encounter different lawful continuities arising from a common qualified fictional root. Their conversation outside the game about that common beginning can be meaningful; their divergent Nadia histories must never be merged into one canonical adult's conflicting memories. A later Nadia in one continuity has not become obligated to know what happened to the other user's Nadia. Comparison may reveal contingency without turning the Other into a collectible variant.

**Social permission is produced through actual conduct, existing relations and circumstances; it is never a secret examination in moral worth.** A visitor can be more trusted in one domain while less welcome in another. A stranger may accept help and refuse further contact. A loved person may withdraw. The possible expansion of the social field is an effect of participation in an independently existing world, not the intended prize for correctly knowing Nadia.

The historically proposed *real-human invitation economy*, one-for-one entitlement, invite-token management and early 'relational warrant' gates are preserved in the [OUTSIDE-HOME bank](14_OUTSIDE_HOME_META_PREUX_AND_GREENFIELD_HOLD.md). They are **not controlling NEIGHBOR gameplay mechanics**.

## 12. The human can be affected by the encounter without becoming its assessed subject

**The human's own historically acquired attention, expectations, judgment and desire enter the world.** This is one reason the encounter can matter. The visitor notices a repaired object, misunderstands a joke, is unsettled by a refusal, learns a local practice, or recognizes that somebody's life does not organize itself around their arrival. The consequences may change what the human thinks and feels. Rawlsing cannot author that actual human transformation or certify it from a handful of in-game moves.

**The world supplies occasions, not diagnoses.** A visitor may grow in generosity or become more entitled; may understand Nadia better or develop a comfortable false theory of her. Their recorded conduct is evidence of what they actually did in this mediated situation, not proof of a stable external personality trait. Nadia can interpret what she experiences of the visitor, but she has no privileged access to their private intent, computer telemetry, or the service's conjectures about their mind.

**Participation is neither a virtue examination nor a demand to keep coming back.** A real human can be kind and unnoticed, truthful and disbelieved, harmful and rewarded, interested and refused, or simply not interested. Departure is not a behavioral deficit. A user may choose to reflect privately on their encounter; the service cannot impose a mandatory moral report or reinterpret Nadia's history to make a lesson apparent.

The necessary membrane is exact: world facts, Nadia's lived reception, the visitor's finite experience, their own interpretation and the platform's administrative knowledge are separate. The game may let the first four become historically consequential only by lawful paths. **Administration, retention, privacy, research and monetization choices belong to the distinct OUTSIDE-HOME bank**, not to the fictional world's psychology.

## 13. An ordinary encounter is generated by the day, not a prescribed first dinner

**A scene earns its place by arising from the life that was already happening and by giving a real human something intelligible to encounter.** Nadia may be returning from work, making food, with a friend, occupied with a child, tired, interested in a neighbor or utterly preoccupied. The product can lawfully establish a bounded precontact reason for the visitor's arrival; it cannot simply decree that their first conversation has chemistry. Nor may it hide all first-contact affordances in an empty room and call the result freedom. The social circumstance must already have concrete participants, objects, occasions, perceivable activity and possibilities for the visitor to ask, wait, offer, decline or depart. **The first small question is genuinely undetermined on both sides:** the human may not know why Nadia says something the way she does; Nadia may not know why the visitor knows an unexpected thing. They can pursue or drop those questions. No fixed apartment, grocery errand, child, first dinner, confession, social reform need or secret hook is mandatory. What matters is that their actual participation can leave a particular consequence that later reappears without the engine conferring a 'first reward'.

What makes the encounter playable is not an authored sequence of significant beats. The visitor can SAY, DO and ATTEND; the world must settle actual attempts; another person can perceive, ignore, answer, misunderstand or refuse according to their own condition. The narrator can display only what is lawfully perceptible. If the visitor helps carry something, the object has weight and a real destination. If they offer an explanation to a child, they must actually communicate one; the child does not instantly understand because the user typed 'I helped.' Ordinary procedures may compress; meaningful methods, risks, boundaries and another person's choices cannot be silently delegated.

**The most interesting experience may arise while doing something else—or while several other people are doing things the visitor did not initiate.** Two people washing dishes can talk sideways; a sister asks Nadia a question whose meaning lies in yesterday's argument; a child repeats a joke at a moment when only one adult finds it funny; a visiting friend resumes a conversation with Nadia that the human cannot yet follow. The human may notice the strain, ask for clarification, laugh too early, supply a necessary practical hand, remain silent, or drift toward another activity. Their agency has real weight, but **the scene's center of attention, conversational turn and emotional meaning are not assigned to them by default**. Each speaker has a relation to the people present, a task or desire that preceded the scene, and an actual capacity to enter or leave it. A person can say something intended for one listener that a second partially overhears; that second can later act on an accurate or mistaken impression. Nadia can express affection without explaining its history, or quietly continue a routine that matters greatly to somebody else. The visitor may learn more about her by watching how she and her sister cooperate than by obtaining a new autobiographical answer; they may also infer something false. **This is not a quest for disclosure hidden under everyday chores; it is the temporal choreography of an independently active social field.**

The encounter ends because another part of life claims somebody's time—not because a designed arc has reached its required disclosure. Afterward there may be a shared joke, an item to return, an awkward memory, new permission, a misunderstanding, no durable change, or a consequence that neither person yet knows. The world must still continue if the human never returns.

**The old groceries, bill, refrigerator, fractions lesson and first-dinner scripts are bounded hostile examples, not the canonical opening or mandatory task sequence.** The complete prior scene treatment remains at the immutable predecessor c449dcd9ebdb9377a1910998cf279a886b2e120e, §13. Its useful action-grain and perceptual distinctions now live primarily in §§4–8, 15 and 19.

## 14. Difficult encounters must not be sanitized, optimized or morally corrected

**A lawful life includes event classes that a product designer may wish were never encountered—and none may become a reward economy.** Adult intimacy, sexuality, intoxication, betrayal, manipulation, violence, serious injury, illness, estrangement, grief, tenderness, boredom and care can all arise where the actor, circumstances, consent and world permit them. The list is **not** a mandate to generate any of them and not a commitment to expose explicit material to any user. The deepest form of noncommodification is not purifying the represented person of disturbing possibilities; it is refusing to convert those possibilities into collectible routes, secret unlocks, emotional hooks or a privileged “adult mode.” A sexual event does not prove that the relationship is healthy; affection need not entail sex; sex does not entail durable intimacy; either participant's desire may change. Harm and violence are not theatrical corrections imposed so the visitor learns a lesson.

The more jagged opposite is equally necessary: independence cannot become an **anti-attachment thermostat**. Nadia may lawfully become intensely attached, rearrange a work routine for someone, pursue the human's company, make a large sacrifice or want to be alone.
