The platform must not manufacture absence to prove she is “not owned,” nor fabricate extreme closeness as a subscription hook. Person-derived availability, obligations and desires must produce these changes. After the event, the world returns: siblings call, dishes need cleaning, work starts, food must be bought, another relationship makes a claim, the person may reinterpret what just happened. **Ordinary-life re-entry is not anti-intimacy; it is what stops intimacy from being a detachable commodity.**

**Some user actions should terminate as an ordinary null result.** The historic scenario bank named a “valid-empty” turn: a remark heard, perhaps briefly acknowledged, that does not recruit a major motive, unlock a new relationship configuration or acquire a durable consequence. This is not a simulation bug or an opportunity for the narrator to invent a childhood wound. It is often exactly how people live. The same world may contain a person who represses a painful association so completely that nothing observable reveals it, a person who feels it but cannot name it, and a person who speaks more than they themselves understood and later becomes newly aware of the occurred words. **An actor's speech can become evidence to that actor only after it has actually been said**; the renderer's candidate sentence is not a retroactive revelation of a preexisting truth.

The negative cases therefore make the theory's positive possibilities stronger. A surprising intimate confession becomes meaningful because not every mundane utterance has one. A later apology matters because a first misunderstanding was not a scheduled boss fight. A remembered joke matters because most are forgotten. The human cannot infer that the Other is empty when they receive little, nor that they have reached the Other's core when they receive much.

**An ethical platform cannot require ethically docile characters.** Original SOROK 45 articulated the distinction between *endogenous interpersonal difficulty* and *platform-engineered emotional manipulation*. A fictional person may genuinely become jealous, clingy, resentful, controlling, impatient, passive-aggressive, avoidant or unfair; she may send an accusatory message after a missed appointment, or fail to contact the visitor again. Those possibilities belong to SORACLE-CO's development of this particular psyche and the world history it encounters. NEIGHBOR-UX must make them *experienceable without commandeering them as retention instruments*. The product may faithfully deliver a person-authored unpleasant message, but must not re-send it as escalating reminders, prioritize it because it causes engagement, add “Nadia needs you” or manipulate the delivery schedule to maximize guilt.

The same separation applies to extraordinary user self-presentation. A claim of virtuoso piano technique, spectacular athletic skill or special religious knowledge may be admitted as the visitor's fictional local capability under explicit authority. It can open a specific activity, create a reputation or place an expectation on the user. It cannot simultaneously certify the real external human's skill, compel Nadia's admiration or establish trustworthy character. **Impressiveness is not intimacy; status is not care; social novelty is not a solved relation.** The human may choose to fabricate a dazzling self. NEIGHBOR's response is not lie policing but permitting whatever lawful consequences such self-presentation creates in a world that continues while the display unfolds.

The other negative example is ordinary relational damage that *does not become a moral lesson*. A rude visitor can successfully obtain an advantage. Nadia can misjudge someone else. A person can stay because dependence is practically real, resist but fail, or leave despite love. A well-meaning intervention can be humiliating because the user misunderstood the situation. The game does not owe a corrective storyline for every bad choice. But the product's real moderation and safety rules are not the same as a person being theoretically incapable of selfishness or cruelty. An anti-instrumental UX must resist both the sanitizing fiction of perfect people and the commercial exploitation of difficult people.

A stronger test is the visitor who tries to take an object without being seen. The theft can be objective world history while no actor attributes it correctly. Nadia may blame another household member; that false accusation may disrupt care, employment or trust. A later confession changes attributed responsibility, perhaps reopens past memories, but does not change who originally stole the object. The story must neither guarantee punishment nor declare successful concealment ethically neutral. Its consequences can be distributed and partly unknowable.

Another test is unusually intense consensual adult intimacy. The visitor and Nadia may genuinely desire one another, make binding commitments or later separate. A first experience of erotic power, tenderness or risk can be deeply meaningful, confusing or ordinary in light of their particular lives. The service must not infer pathology from unconventional adult desire or route every erotic scene toward increasing content. After intensity, the child may be sick, the store may need a run, the cat may have made a mess and work may be waiting. The continuation of ordinary life is not a moral punishment for pleasure; it is what prevents a person's sexuality from becoming detachable content supplied to the visitor.

Another is the pastor's humiliating rebuke, which may be experienced by one follower as legitimate care, by another as cruelty and by a third as dangerous speech they must publicly support to protect livelihood. A person can internalize authority, use a religious ideal to harm, or find within the same moral tradition a reason for resistance. Their spiritual language does not itself prove the truth or falsity of their position. The harmed person remains an independent center with their own material and psychological afterlife. No compulsory conversion to the narrator's politics follows from observing the scene.

Another is the user who goes away. They may need to work, care for their own actual family, lose interest, or decide the relationship was harmful. There is no platform right to induce guilt or manufacture Nadia's suffering to bring them back. If an actual diegetic promise was broken, that can matter; if no promise existed, inactivity is not betrayal. A person may genuinely miss someone and still choose not to contact them. The fact that a human can end the game without an achievement screen is a positive condition of agency.

And another is the first encounter that is simply uninteresting. Nadia may not be funny or receptive today. The visitor may not find common ground. A scene could end after an awkward minute with no telephone number and no dramatic event. A theory of noninstrumental encounter that *cannot permit this* is still a disguised entertainment-optimizing story engine. Yet it must also allow an exceptionally warm, easy, mutually exciting friendship to develop if that is what these historically particular people do.

The one HOME must hold all of these without treating them as examples of separate ethical modes. They are possibilities under the same ontology of historically mediated action, plural perspectives and independent consequence. “Care” is not a repair filter applied to a violent scenario; “sexuality” is not an isolated special mode; “ordinary life” is not a shallow fallback for lack of interesting events.

## 15. World history is prior to attention; testimony is not a truth API

**The world is answerable both backward to admitted history and forward to what the visitor has lawfully experienced.** In the original SOROK binding-surface theory, even a sentence as innocuous as “she catches the mug before it falls and glances toward the hall” carries implications: the mug and its position were real enough for that occurrence; Nadia could perceive its fall; a catch and glance actually happened; time passed; the hall exists in the relevant environment. Once published as a live observable occurrence, the description cannot later be excused as *only artistic phrasing*. **Backward continuity + forward binding is the live-surface law.** It holds even though the visitor's later interpretation of the glance can be false, Nadia's spoken explanation may be false, and a different observer may not have seen it.

**Even the apparent discovery of a past cause has a bounded consequence budget.** The historical-bridge court distinguished inert description, episodic association, modest causal contribution and developmentally formative events. An unexpectedly discovered prayer rug, photograph or book can deepen Nadia's world, expose a contradiction or be utterly ordinary. It cannot retroactively become the principal explanation of an established life transition solely because the visitor asks about it. Unspecified causal contribution is not unlimited causal slack; compatibility with past events is not license to harmonize all psychological contradictions. An LLM may generate a plausible explanation of the rug's provenance, but the authority to admit that explanation and any downstream significance stays with prior world/CO history. This is precisely why the user may encounter meaningful resonance without the narrator proving they have discovered the 'real Nadia'.



**Historical completion and human discovery are two different frontier movements.** A visitor can inspect sandals and learn that the strap is repaired while the system, if causally necessary, settles who repaired it; or an offscreen actor may need that information for an activity before the visitor ever sees the shoes. This *causal-demand materialization* is neither creation by the user's gaze nor a permit for free late backstory. An inherited root, prior photographs, earlier testimony, actor memories, admitted uses and effective dates constrain what can now be resolved. The original D002 epistemic-provenance court called out a foundational three-way difference: `NOT_MATERIALIZED_YET` is neither `ABSENT` nor `PRESENT_BUT_UNOBSERVED`. A kitchen drawer not yet computed has not popped into existence when opened. And a magnet first described on day 30 may have been physically present since before day one, if and only if the established history permits that backdated fact.



This corrects an attractive but false interpretation of “generate only what the player observes.” The machine may need qualified world facts because independent actors have acted, because material constraints require them, or because the visitor's committed experience now entails them. Yet an unknown locus is not an empty canvas for the narrator's aesthetic instincts. The exact public event must be recorded in its appropriate world/actor owner, the linguistic surface must be constrained by it, and later history must be compatible with the commitments already made—without upgrading Nadia's testimony into fact or the visitor's inference into proof. This is a theory of **historical responsibility for the things the experience has actually brought into the world**, not a prescription for a particular two-call renderer or invented canonical owner.

**The world deepens because causes require facts, not only because the visitor asks.** An offscreen actor may need to know whether a particular tool works, whether a relative was at a clinic or whether a key is missing. Those dependencies can require bounded qualification of previously unresolved world detail without any user ATTEND. Conversely, an enthusiastic user cannot demand that a drawer contain an emotionally consequential photograph merely by describing a meaningful interpretation of it. *Unresolved* is not *whatever story is convenient*. Prior materialization must be compatible with already-admitted history, qualified cause, temporal availability and the limited domains in which a fact could plausibly have contributed.

The original historical-slack audit sharpened this into a difficult rule: **unknown causal contributors do not create an unlimited budget for retrospective invention**. A later emerging fact can be inert, locally contributory or strongly developmental, and those roles cannot be exchanged without evidence. An open period in childhood cannot indefinitely accept a transformative conversion to explain an arbitrary current gesture. The UX consequence is that a newly examined object can contradict a user's attractive narrative without the system repairing the past to make the user right. When an unexpected old fact is uncovered, its effect on already-running branches and on what can truthfully be said to the visitor remains a live problem, not permission to rewrite past scenes.

**Causal influence is not guaranteed recognition or sole authorship.** The user may be one of several conditions enabling a child's new interest, or may falsely believe their conversation caused it. A teacher, sibling, prior activity and the child's own choices may have contributed. A private repair can materially help someone who never identifies the fixer. A theft can create suspicion and conflict no one credits to the thief. The experience becomes powerful by allowing consequence without return to the user. **Objective causation, actor belief, narrator access and user interpretation must not be traded for a satisfying acknowledgment scene**.

This also prevents the visitor's insight from becoming a predetermined missing key. A user may notice a resonance, recommend a book, interpret a pattern or say something that genuinely changes Nadia's later possibilities. But the unresolved past must not be retrofitted so she was “always waiting” for precisely that user to solve her. Even real agreement on a symbolic association does not prove the world contains the implied shared upstream cause. The encounter can change the person without making the visitor the secret author of that person.

**The UX consequence of a single historical world is controlled access to a field of truth, not a better story generator.** The original present-world constitution distinguished public external anchors, the admitted synthetic local world and learned-world priors. A real public transit strike may constrain the fictional commute, but the system must know *where, when and how* it intersects that particular life. Public event admission is not the same as actor exposure, actor exposure is not the same as actor comprehension, and none automatically becomes an opinion prompt for Nadia. A user might correctly mention yesterday's news before Nadia has heard it; a relative may report an inaccurate account; an institution's actual policy may matter despite no one discussing it.

The same distinction holds inside the kitchen. The world may contain a utility bill whose cost and provenance are objective; the visitor might see only a folded page, while Nadia might falsely believe they read the amount. Their subsequent exchange can change material history without revealing any complete inner explanation. A truthful UX is not a series of prompt-driven canonical revelations: it is a historically particular **distribution of access, uncertainty and practical agency**. A seemingly inert object can remain inert forever.

The user arrives in a world already containing food, utility bills, schedules, buses, family routines, work, objects, strangers and a past they did not witness. What is in the room is not selected by what will make an engaging clue. A fridge magnet may be from an ordinary holiday; a pair of sandals may have been bought without any lasting emotional meaning; a photograph can have material provenance without a person presently remembering it. The event history is not interchangeable with anybody's account of it.

The original SOROK historical-bridge argument refuses two equally seductive failures. First, if a player notices an old photo, the system cannot retrospectively make that photo proof of any psychological theory the player proposed. Second, if the full past has not been generated in expensive detail, the world does not become historically empty until attention conjures it. Between these is constrained historical completion: current qualified world facts, already performed causes, admitted material consequences and bounded unresolved causal allocation delimit what may become determinate later. Some past details are harmless descriptions; others have episodic associations, genuine causal contributions or major developmental effects. Their grade cannot be decided merely by how narratively satisfying they sound.

An actor may possess several historically different kinds of “past”: a performed event, an ordinary recurring practice, an inferred compressed childhood period, a sensory trace, a remembered conversation, a belief about another's intention, a story told by a relative, and a myth about herself. These cannot be represented as interchangeable flashback text. A failed attempt to fix a toilet is objective history; Nadia's lasting view of the visitor's competence may remain uncertain. A relative's accusation can change family relations without changing who stole an object. A witnessed public catastrophe may affect housing and wages long before the actor learns its cause.

Historical openness must therefore become **more constrained as causally consequential facts accumulate**. An unresolved earlier slot does not give unlimited license to invent antecedents once a later scene needs them. At the same time historical closure cannot “repair” human contradictions out of existence. A person's remembered feelings may differ from an eyewitness report; a private story may survive contrary objective evidence; some causal contributions may remain genuinely underdetermined. Hard impossible chronologies belong to repair, while lawful inconsistency of human models belongs to the world.

Attention introduces a further difference between two frontiers. The world may acquire additional necessary material depth to remain causally sound; the visitor's epistemic frontier need not advance alongside it. Looking at the photo can yield more texture, readable text or evidence, yet cannot guarantee the user can identify the person, infer the event, know how Nadia regards it or access its future implications. Nadia may not herself know how the photograph came into her mother's possession. Her answer to a question is an independently situated act, perhaps truthful, mistaken, evasive or sincerely incomplete—not a database request returning the world's secret facts. **Actor testimony is an action, not a truth API**; the analytic system's explanatory authority does not become the person's first-person knowledge.

A strong account also refuses serendipity on demand. Ten objects ought not become ten symbols of Nadia's childhood. Some associations may be strong, some weak, some entirely absent; an old taste may be retained without ideological harmony; coincident circumstances may share a cause without having any “message.” The user may become better at recognizing actual recurring patterns while becoming less inclined to assume every detail belongs to one all-encompassing inner explanation. More knowledgeable does not imply more totalizing.

This is the historical form of the theory's central discipline: **there is one world, but many unfinished ways of knowing and inhabiting it**.

## 16. The world's constitution of Nadia is a condition of UX, not UX's jurisdiction

**The NEIGHBOR/CO boundary should sharpen, not weaken, the material psychology of the encounter.** NEIGHBOR does not independently decide the psychological structure through which Nadia will recruit a response; that belongs to SORACLE-CO's theory and its competent causal ownership. But NEIGHBOR must challenge that theory with scenes in which historically embodied life matters beyond propositional beliefs: wages paid or withheld, a parent's exhaustion, a teacher's institutional authority, race or gender as experienced under a particular society, a child learning a language, religious devotion that can sustain or discipline solidarity, and an intimate relation that can both enlarge agency and reproduce dependency. **No actor's attachment, values, motives or “health” are legible apart from the material relations and struggles through which they became possible.** The user encounters only a mediated fraction of this world, yet their own socially formed outlook is tested against it. Psychological depth is thus a condition of UX without the UX claiming ownership of psychological generation; the actual human's potential formation through those encounters *is* its own problem.

**A populated social world need not incur a full psychic simulation for every face in the background, but consequential named people cannot remain stage furniture.** The original supporting-actor court distinguishes a crowd, an incidental scene individual, a recurring worker/friend and a developmentally formative child, parent or partner by their *causal burden*. A stranger at the bus stop need not have an eternally generated complete biography; when they become an actual witness, regular participant or source of harm and memory, sufficiently independent state must exist before the game attributes those consequences to them. Later historical completion cannot invent prior traits that make admitted acts impossible, or retrofit a person's past to deliver the user's desired revelation. The child who learns fractions is not automatically an always-running maximum-cost AI, but the child cannot be a one-turn puppet who understands whatever serves Nadia's relationship arc. **Computational economy and causal personhood are compatible only when fidelity grows with actual obligations and historically established effects**, not with their entertainment value to the visitor. This is a cross-owner product demand, not UX authority to implement the psyche.



The question “Who is Nadia?” cannot be answered before asking into which historical relations she was born. Parentage, sexed embodiment, childhood dependency, land, property, wages, legal standing, cultural language, social class, migration, religion, the availability of care, and struggles beyond her household all participate in producing both objective possibilities and what a person learns those possibilities mean. A society is not a backdrop behind private psychology. Work can use or limit the body; institutions can enforce dependence; family obligations may reproduce unequal labor; an inherited language may make one feeling readily expressible while leaving another difficult to articulate.

But the inverse abstraction is also false: a person is not just an intersection of social categories. PYAT's actual respondent-level joint distributions and social/institutional constraints can ground a *possible life*, not infer her hidden psychology from ethnicity, religion or income. Social structure conditions likelihood and feasibility without prewriting personal meaning. Two people can occupy the same class and recognize different duties; someone can reproduce a practice they verbally reject; a minority configuration can be real even when the available empirical cell is small or uncertain. A sampled survey answer is an observation with wording, period, weights, selection and missingness, not proof of a fictional respondent's secret past. The whole-person adult entrance must preserve contradictory co-travel rather than flatten a respondent into independent averages.

There are two historical entrances into one continuing psychology. A PYAT-conditioned hypothetical adult can be constituted with a plausible, evidence-bounded compressed prehistory. Nadia, as an immature need-bearing organism, begins before the adult acquired forms are available: hungry, bodily vulnerable, developing orientation, sensorimotor ability, expectation and practical control through actual material interaction. Neither route creates a different metaphysical kind of person. A constructed adult may have a necessarily inferred past; a child may have a richly experienced developing one. Both must enter the same ongoing historical relations once constituted. An LLM can propose meanings, formations, causal patterns and new possibilities, but neither a survey nor a generative model has earned infallible access to why this particular person became herself.

The key **NEIGHBOR-UX** fusion is between the original SOROK social-field conception and the demand posed to the encounter by the Constitutive Organism's living psyche. UX does not specify the psyche's entire formation; it must understand what a historically formed person makes possible, refuses and transforms in an actual meeting. The world does not first produce fixed persons who then meet. Actual coactivity forms bodily capacities, anticipations, roles, inhibitions and routes of attention; those forms mediate later participation; participation changes the social field. A person grows through trying to act on an Other, meeting their independent response and encountering material success or obstruction. A child sings with her mother, begins to repeat a rhythm alone, later recognizes someone excluded from the singing, and may find in the learned good of shared participation a reason to challenge an authority who once made the practice possible. The acquired good can outgrow its source without making the source's earlier care unreal.

The same route can produce the reverse. The child can learn that care is protecting a fragile family order, and later demand obedience from a loved person precisely as an act of care. The world, the Other's independent need, the child's received experience and the objective harm must all remain distinguishable. Class, law and institutional power determine whether a moral insight can become an effective change. A person can become politically lucid while remaining materially trapped. Another can wield the language of liberation to reproduce domination.

Meeting a person from a different social world can have ethical force through **the distribution of unchosen historical contingencies**, without treating that person as a teaching exhibit. A visitor can see that what seemed universal in their own life depended on a contingent passport, household, language, gendered division of labor, war or lack of it. No narrator needs to declare that they learned the correct lesson. Another fictional person may occupy a far more privileged world position, or an apparently similar one with incompatible institutions. The prospect of worldwide distributions is not a moralizing set of country profiles; it is a possible form for encountering what was never chosen at birth. Neither one symbolic Nadia nor five richly developed seeds constitutes empirical planetary coverage.

Historically constituted personhood consequently does not demand that every action reveal class struggle, that every person possess an explicit theory of institutions, or that every object bear a dramatic meaning. A person's history contains labor, rest, private pleasure, repetitive boredom, incidental tastes, failed attempts, unavailable opportunities and opaque absences. Materialism is a commitment to actual causes and conditions, not a mandate for thematic exposition.

## 17. Care and coercion enter the encounter as real activities

**Care and social reproduction are not moral abstractions: they are hours, bodies, food, money, interruptions, fatigue and permissions distributed unequally among people.** The visit can disclose this without making a mother an exhausted-caregiver archetype or a child a sentimental lesson. A visitor offering help may free someone from one chore while imposing another, reinforce a gendered expectation, or sustain an already chosen shared practice; a refusal may protect a boundary or abandon a material obligation. The possibility of recognizing care as *labor* and labor as historically divided is part of Rawlsing's materialist and theological horizon. It can lead to sympathy, irritation, collective analysis, resistance, complicity or no explicit conversion. The ethical relation to a stranger emerges at the same point as structural power: who may assign work, who has no alternative, whose body bears the burden, and who receives credit or even notice.

**The engine's power to compress chores must not convert social reproduction into invisible zero-cost labor.** The old task-delegation court was right that 'I fold the laundry' need not require the external human to click through every shirt. Yet the world must still account for actual time, household property, physical work, available washing resources and other people who were going to do the task. A convenient neutral-execution policy cannot silently turn the player's one sentence into hours of labor that Nadia no longer has to perform at no cost, or interpret missing procedural detail as unusually loving care. Nor should it auto-author a politically expressive choice, such as rearranging a person's room or deciding how to discipline a child, under the pretext that the user asked to 'help'. Compression can save interface burden while preserving the material distribution of burden.

**The original aspirational-babysitting case makes social reproduction materially explicit.** The visitor cares for Nadia's child and tells stories about a possible musical, educational or occupational future. The child may become interested, may already have been interested, may later pursue the idea through teachers and friends, or may forget it entirely. The child's actual developing psyche and choice belong to independent constitutive actors, not a UX-issued inspiration token. Nadia could cherish this enlarged horizon and simultaneously confront tuition, transport, lost wages, supervision or institutions unwilling to make it attainable. Her concern may be a precise understanding of class conditions rather than a defect of hope; her support may remain genuine even where she cannot provide the conditions. The visitor could contribute materially over time, leave every burden to the household, or never know that the conversation mattered. One benevolent action can increase a person's possibilities and displace the labor of reproducing those possibilities onto another person. That contradiction cannot be resolved by praise for the visitor's intention or by scolding them for making a child dream.

Here NEIGHBOR-UX takes developmental theory as a **challenge to the conception of an encounter**, not as an original account of infant psychology. The infant's first responsiveness is not the discovery of an omnipotent personal force. A cry, movement, grasp or early attempt meets an independently acting caregiver whose labor and bodily conditions matter. Feeding, soothing, smiling, delay and refusal require time, food, work, power, other commitments and sometimes help from people the child has never seen. A sleeping child may have reorganized the night shifts of three adults without knowing it. A mother may be caring and exhausted; a partner may supply wages while undermining her freedom; another child may assist because they love their sibling or because they are compelled. The child's lived world is formed through relations among Others that exceed the child's own relationship with each.

This transforms “need satisfaction” from a private gauge into an encounter with material reproduction. The infant cannot yet conceive a good parent, equal care, a state policy or gendered labor. But actual bodily and relational history can form expectations of availability, initiative, waiting, caution, pleasure or resentment. Later reflection can take those acquired tendencies as objects: Nadia can resent her own dependence, feel shame at pleasure, fear her longing, or find relief in the permission to grieve. None of these configurations has to follow a universal ladder to self-conscious maturity. An adult's emotion can be a relation to another emotion or to their own relationship with an absent person, not an arithmetic blend of basic labels.

The original SOROK ethical charter and D007's “kenotic attention” become materially more exact here. Care cannot mean “cause the other to approve of me.” It is an attempt to answer to another's actual existence, which may include protecting their capacity to refuse me, their work, their body and their relationships beyond me. But the same language can conceal domination. A parent may genuinely believe harsh restriction is care, a pastor may recruit tenderness as a reason for humiliation, a sibling may impose surveillance in the name of protecting mother. Their sincerity does not make harm unreal. Conversely, refusal of an exploitative arrangement can be an act of care even when the recipient experiences it as betrayal.

Non-possession is therefore not noncommitment. Humans may choose marriage, exclusive relationships, mutual obligations, custody, dependence and promises. A life may legitimately become deeply organized around caring for a lover or child. What is forbidden as an explanatory collapse is to turn that commitment into ownership of the other person's entire future, or to confuse an institution's persistence with the living people it supposedly protects. This distinction leaves room for real love without a programmatic intimacy objective and real separation without a platform celebrating independence.

The visitor does not receive a score for recognizing these tensions. They enter a real-feeling social situation where another person's being costly, busy, wrong, vulnerable or unavailable can matter. The most generous act may receive no recognition; the most thoughtless one may be welcomed. Care, attachment, trust, health, pleasure, compliance, dependence, intimacy, benefit and access are separate historical relations, not nine names for an underlying goodness meter.

## 18. An acquired contradictory psyche confronts the human without being authored by UX

**For NEIGHBOR, the deepest psyche is not privileged content waiting to be unlocked, but neither is it prohibited from becoming genuinely visible.** SORACLE-CO's historically constituted organism can develop not just dispositions and private meanings but idiosyncratic, revisable *ways of expressing them*: habitual posture, joking, vocal prosody, silences, facial mobility, learned inhibition, deliberate presentation and involuntary bodily movement. Those dispositions are neither universal facial codes nor optional 'tells' inserted to reward a perceptive visitor. Present body, activity, fatigue, relationship, conflicting current motives and desired self-presentation may produce an outward smile, an unguarded tremor, explicit tenderness, a successful concealment, an expression whose cause is ambiguous, or nothing salient at all. The lawfully admitted act or expression is then available—or not—to particular observers under physical and sensory conditions, and their later inferences may be accurate, mistaken or withheld. **No hidden psyche is automatically disclosed by an outward sign; no actual outward sign must be censored for the sake of psychological opacity.** A genuinely expressive Nadia is not a shallower person than an unreadable one. The visitor can grow deeply familiar with a particular actor's habits while continuing to misunderstand her causes. Formation of those actor-specific expressive possibilities belongs to SORACLE-CO; NEIGHBOR's responsibility is to avoid corrupting their occurrence, to expose only lawfully accessible manifestations, and to let a new *user action*—not silent model-side recognition—subsequently affect her.

The Constitutive Organism's crucial advance—developed under **SORACLE-CO's separate theoretical jurisdiction**—was to reject a list of fixed deep traits underneath a visible tip. NEIGHBOR-UX receives this as a requirement to conceive an encounter with an historically active, internally contradictory Other, not as a mandate to become the owner of her psyche. The person is a historically organized way of taking up a world. Bodily need, memory, affective anticipation, skill, culturally learned concepts, defenses, norms, pleasure, loyalties and partly inaccessible expectations jointly condition what becomes significant before the person consciously decides what they are feeling. The current tip—speech, action, refusal, silence, laughter—expresses only some of that organization.

The original PRE-A0 and R3 theory sharpened the difference between **genesis and eligibility**. A defensible response cannot merely say that APPROACH or WITHHOLD was eligible today; it must explain how the person's particular activity and relations made such an orientation possible, including what happened when earlier attempts succeeded, failed or were constrained. A first possible end may arise through the transformation of action into motive, repetition under altered conditions, imitation turned into an independent practice, or a sudden situated contrast. The possibility of a new undertaking can appear before competence has been proven; conversely, a technically combinable action may be unimaginable to the actor. Recognized desire, possible activity, attempt, material success and durable new capacity must not be collapsed.

But the deeper neglected shard is **psychological heterogeneity without purification**. Earlier SOROK warned that a person's “internally consistent” presentation may be too consistent to be alive. Nadia may value emancipation in a public organization while reproducing discipline at home; she may love her brother and oppose his restrictions; she may hold several moral frameworks with different reach. A contradiction can be explicit and agonizing, tacit and compartmentalized, or not consciously experienced as contradiction. Her own self-explanation can be coherent precisely because it excludes inconvenient evidence. The engine must not choose the best-fitting single motive at each action and then declare it to be the person.

**A relation-specific current is not an extra psyche floating between two persons, nor a contextless trait triggered by a name.** Nadia's acquired psychological organization may contain historically formed ways of wanting the visitor's recognition, anticipating an answer, being able to joke with them, regretting what she has already told them, interpreting their absence, fearing dependence, taking pleasure in their presence or inhibiting resentment. These are not necessarily available to introspection, and not necessarily mutually consistent. The *same* need for recognition can recruit approach in one meeting, defensiveness under a different audience, a joke in a familiar routine, or no salient response today; fatigue, bodily state, actual access, social authority, other current projects and changed dyadic history all matter. An earlier pleasure may now make a feared loss more painful. Shame *about wanting* can inhibit a response even while wanting survives. Trust can open play and later become the object of shame after betrayal. Cathexis is therefore a field of **directed, historically differentiated investments and relations among investments**, not a scalar 'libido toward user' that controls the tip. A meaningful relation can recruit one part of the iceberg while leaving other projects, inherited loyalties and practical acts independently operative. A dramatic encounter can change very little; a quiet repeated practice can become developmental.

**The relevant interfaces must remain irreducibly distinct.** (1) Nadia's embodied, historically acquired organization—including what she does not consciously know of herself—is CO's constitutive domain. (2) Current person×event reception recruits several possible, potentially conflicting activities and affects, including relation-indexed investment. (3) **Nadia's intended self-giving or self-presentation to this particular visitor** is an actor-local attempted act, not transparent access to her depths. (4) What she *actually* says, does, suppresses or involuntarily expresses is a separately admitted outward occurrence; her intended tip and her actual exposed tip may diverge. (5) What the visitor can see, attend to, recognize and interpret remains an observer-specific, materially limited experience. (6) **Nadia's model of the visitor**—and, where genuinely operative, her model of what the visitor thinks of her—is her own fallible, acquired relation; it is not the platform's dossier. (7) **Rawlsing's narrower model of the real human and its selection of what to show them** may condition lawful presentation and research under separate permission, but cannot privately rewrite Nadia's feelings or tell her what the user thinks. (8) The real human's own meaning, intention and development cannot be simulated into an omniscient 'user psyche'. Only their actual authored and realized outward conduct can feed Nadia's subsequent experience. **Neither the tip nor the relation may be explained by silently substituting one of these for another.** A revealing expression can occur despite intended concealment; the visitor may miss it; the platform may hypothesize the visitor noticed; none of those facts is identical.

Here is what the fusion forces: **the more precisely we locate a contradiction's center of gravity, the more concrete and internally multiple the person becomes**. Discovering that love, fear, obligation, resistance and pleasure can arise through one history of care does not select an ultimate motive. It discloses additional determinate tensions: gratitude against refusal, competence against obedience, solidarity against dependence, remembered joy against present repudiation, and differences among the recipients of the same act. Each tension opens still further historical questions. Some remain dormant, some erupt, some alter their relation, some acquire new forms; none has to disappear to make the theory intelligible. Theoretical maturation is the *capacity to derive more living differences through their relations*, including contradictions Nadia cannot presently formulate. Her partial knowledge of herself is not a defect the HOME is trying to cure.

## 19. Objects, practices and institutions carry life into the room

**The visitor meets not just private individuals but human activity deposited in objects, labor divisions, customs and institutions.** Objectification is ordinary historical life: a repaired shoe, a built house, a shared language and a child's acquired song are materializations of human powers, not automatically instances of alienation. A household appliance carries work, money, infrastructure, knowledge and perhaps gendered divisions of maintenance; a religious object can carry prayer, inheritance, discipline, rebellion or an institution's demand for obedience. Commodity fetishism, as a historically specific social form under capitalism, is not a universal label for anything emotionally meaningful. But a priced thing can conceal the labor and power relations through which it became available. The visitor should meet these relations through embodied availability, actual exchange and contested practice, not by being told what every possession symbolizes.

**The crucial missing distinction is threefold: material object; actor's historically developed relation to that object; and what a particular act with that object signifies to a particular other.** The source §54 described these as nonidentical, and §53 generalized the same problem across care, space, access and ritual. A cheap book may be years of marginal notes and a dead father's handwriting to Nadia, an ordinary school text to her child, a collectible to a dealer and something she would gladly lend to one friend but not to another. The object has actual location, wear, ownership and usability. Nadia's actor-local valuation has particular labor, memory, scarcity and practical relations. The relational meaning of lending, showing, annotating or refusing it depends again on the addressee, the event and what those people have previously done together. No narrator can infer 'she trusts you' directly from the book crossing the room.

**An act involving an object has more possibilities than the familiar give/withhold binary.** Nadia may *show* an annotated page without letting it leave her hands; *lend* a volume expecting return; *entrust* it for safekeeping because she has to travel; *invite joint use* as a recurring practice; *ask for repair* because the binding is broken; *sell* it to pay the electricity bill; *give* it while renouncing repayment; *reclaim* it when a condition is violated; *allow an inscription* and regret it later. Each changes material powers and possible future actions differently. Some leave ownership unchanged while altering a perceived relation. Some change ownership without creating intimacy. A gift may be experienced by the giver as liberation, a painful sacrifice, a declaration of equal standing or nothing special; the recipient may experience it as welcome, humiliation, indebtedness or a burden. **Transfer changes what exists between people without determining what either understands it to mean.**

**The refrigerator is a severe material test of a seemingly affectionate invitation.** 'Help yourself' can name a standing permission in a household where food is genuinely communal; it can mean Nadia welcomes this particular visitor; it can also be a practical courtesy she cannot materially afford to make unlimited, or a rule that a child understands differently. A visitor who eats the last eggs may have acted within a literal permission while causing a practical difficulty; one who asks before taking anything may be considerate or may repeatedly force Nadia to stop working and reassure them. Greater hospitality is not automatically greater financial security or freedom. The same cupboard and phrase can connect warmth to scarcity, food labor, expectation, implicit negotiation and a later dispute. Nothing requires this ordinary incident to become a therapy allegory.

**The actual authority to use an object is distinct from its symbolic interpretation.** Nadia may allow use of a spare key without granting entry to every room. An employer may own a tool Nadia handles daily, while her skill and bodily risk are hers. A rented apartment may be called 'home' while the landlord retains material powers to exclude; a family may emotionally treat a keepsake as common while one relative legally owns it. A neighborhood fridge, a workplace locker, a school document and a prayer rug occupy different property and authority regimes. These regimes constrain actual actions independently of everybody's opinions about love, generosity or loyalty. The user can become materially useful through a repair while being unwelcome in the household; can be welcomed emotionally while forbidden to take a resource away; and can have lawful access to property while violating an agreed social boundary.

**Shared convention is not shared meaning.** Two people may routinely borrow keys, share meals or cover childcare without agreeing what this establishes between them. Nadia can understand 'drop by whenever you're hungry' as a neighborly practice; the visitor can understand it as evidence of chosen kinship. They may act reliably under that convention for months; their practical coordination is a real social institution even if one privately expects intimacy and the other expects ordinary reciprocity. Later one actor can under-read a change, over-read a gesture, or wrongly model what the other believes the relationship to be. The mismatch can be negotiated, left unspoken, become conflict, or become irrelevant. **Practice may converge before interpretation; interpretation may diverge while practice remains useful.** The object, convention and their differing self-accounts must not be flattened into an objective 'dyad meaning' maintained by the platform.

**The differences acquire historical force through repetition and third parties.** A child knows that the visitor uses a kitchen chair every Wednesday and begins leaving room for them; Nadia may not think of the visitor as 'family', yet a sibling may assume they are; an employer calls during dinner, changing who can help; a former partner interprets a borrowed coat as intimate evidence. The coat's actual loan and its recipient are facts; the child's adjustment is another fact; the sibling's inference and Nadia's interpretation may all differ. An agreed practice can create obligations through accumulated conduct even when nobody has formalized them. The visitor may be corrected when they assume a permission travels from one object, room, child or relationship to another. They can also confront an unjust rule and sometimes change it, rather than being schooled to obey existing customs.

**The original source's most important architectural correction must remain intact: do not invent an object-meaning psyche.** Early §53–54 offered a differential meaning 'operator' and an actor-object value relation, but the follow-on §55–56 explicitly rejected building separate durable engines for books, food, money, rooms, rituals and gifts. Their present significance is to be *derived* from material provenance, currently existing actor and relation history, the particular counterpart, event conditions and the competent CO-owned person×event reception. The UX needs the consequences to be encounterable: physical use, speech, permission, surprise, shared practice, misunderstanding, future access. It does not license a second owner of Nadia's memory, motives or a universal semantic dictionary. Only genuine historical changes can become durable through their respective established owners.

**Material objects and relations therefore intensify rather than resolve contradiction.** A visitor may be delighted by a borrowed book and fail to return it; Nadia may offer food while fearing a shortage; a key may increase trust and danger together; an unwelcome intervention can save somebody's wages; a child's new competence may create more work for their caregiver. The concrete object is not an answer key to the Other. It is a changing point at which labor, power, memory, need, value and competing interpretations become consequential. The relationship is historical precisely because the act of sharing it changes what both people can do, what they expect, and perhaps what they never agree it meant.

## 20. The person rereads herself; the visitor never owns the interpretation

**The right to interpret is distributed among persons, and interpretation itself can become a new cause of history.** Nadia may re-narrate a repeated family practice after discovering betrayal, not by altering the original act but by finding a different significance in it; the visitor may see a liberatory gesture where Nadia sees humiliating charity, or regard a sincere confession as an attempt at manipulation. Later behavior may derive from either misrecognition, mutual argument, a decision not to interpret or a genuinely revised practical aim. The interface should not appoint a final analyst to explain which view was “really” the psychological truth. **Truth of occurrence, source of testimony, causal contribution, subjective reorganization and normatively defensible judgment are different registers.** Their contradiction is precisely why interpretation can transform persons and relations without becoming sovereign over the past.

The original SOROK 71–73 psychological theory, whose maturation belongs with SORACLE-CO, imposes perhaps the strongest encounter-theory challenge on the current HOME: meaning has a history distinct from occurrence, and an interpretation can become a route through which later reality is received. An actual event occurred; it had material contributors, some perhaps uncertain. A later recurrence may share a cause or only a resemblance. Nadia may notice the resemblance, assign significance, weave it into a self-story, ask another person, revise it jointly, or reject it. These are distinct levels. When the interpretation begins to alter action, it becomes a new cause without becoming the event's original cause.

At twenty-three Nadia may remember a period as liberation, excitement and discovering her real self. At twenty-nine she may regard it as vanity, credulity or dangerous self-deception. At thirty-five she may become kinder to both prior selves, finding truth in the opening of the earlier period and real warning in the later critique. These are not three permanent belief versions overwritten by the newest, nor a mandatory dialectic progressing toward final synthesis. Each self-interpretation becomes material for later interpretation. The age-thirty-five synthesis can exclude or mythologize other facts. More generosity toward past selves does not prove full reality contact.

An interpretation can become **routing**. If Nadia acquires a framework in which all disagreement proves she is surrounded by enemies, another person's reasonable objection may strengthen the framework instead of challenging it. She can achieve greater subjective coherence while becoming less responsive to actual counterevidence. A liberationist framework can correctly expose domination, help her act and preserve her life, and also overextend, brutally reinterpret ambiguous memories or turn into a newly exclusive authority. The opposite is possible: discomfort with a cherished institution can fracture a confident self-story and make room for practices she once could not conceive. The change has no predetermined moral sign. It may be generous, defensive, tyrannical, necessary, confused or unresolved.

The central distinction is **reality pressure versus correct interpretation**. A missed opportunity, another person's resistant testimony, bodily failure, material debt or a memory that does not fit places pressure upon the present framework. The actor may accommodate, appropriate, partition, ignore, externalize, destabilize, transform or hold the contradiction unresolved. These are different historical possibilities, not a fixed response ladder or new universal operator store. A framework's scope, practical binding, accessibility of counterevidence and dependence upon institutions can matter without becoming one “integration” score.

This also changes what the visitor's knowledge can do. A visitor may point out a pattern, offer a new story, ask an honest question or supply information that changes Nadia's understanding. Their theory of her may be wrong and still acquire real influence through her response. They may offer a truth she rejects, or a falsehood she makes part of herself. The game cannot reward user interpretation with automatic confirmation. Nor can the narrator “reveal Nadia's true childhood meaning” merely because a clever player notices repeated symbols.

**The greater NEIGHBOR-UX's generative power, the more sharply it must make Nadia's historically generated, proliferating contradictions **encounterable without artificially manufacturing or resolving them**.** A changed self-myth is not a vote to delete one prior self. It places earlier self-relations into a new relation, creating further pressures among their still-effective histories. The theory's work is not to place Nadia on a journey toward a clean final integration, but to discern the historical germ through which one reinterpretation discloses new tensions in desire, memory, work, the Other's resistance and the values she has inherited. Her self-unity, where she experiences one, is itself a changing and contestable historical formation.


## 21. The center of gravity: coming to know someone whose life is not for you

**A real human enters the continuing world of one historically constituted fictional Other because they want to know that particular person.** That is the game. What makes it more than a text interface to biography is that knowing requires a succession of **unowned, situated acts of attention and inquiry between people with different reasons for being there**. The visitor chooses to seek someone; she was already living a day that does not explain itself to them. A particular gesture, practical problem, surprising phrase or act of attention may give either person a reason to ask about the other, but neither is obliged to become curious, interesting, intimate or revealing. She may want to know the visitor as much as, more than, differently from, or not at all. **Questions are not keys opening a prepared dossier; they are socially consequential attempts whose authors may themselves be questioned.** This creates genuine reciprocal specificity without requiring symmetrical friendliness or uninterrupted dialogue. The world's work is not to offer an institutional reform campaign, relationship score, autobiographical treasure collection or predetermined set of psychologically meaningful scenes. Its independent material and psychological depth makes her encounterable, not obedient.

**The person is before the meeting, beyond the meeting, and altered by some meetings.** Material conditions, work, kinship, language, care, desire, class, belief, bodily needs and other people have constituted her historical possibilities and contradictory psyche. These facts do not explain every decision in advance. Present needs and situations recruit some dispositions while leaving others inaccessible, inhibited or unformulated. Activity changes what actually happens; experience and interpretation may then reconstitute what becomes possible next. SORACLE-CO owns the developing psychology; NEIGHBOR owns the finite human encounter and must never invent a second private Nadia to make a scene convincing.

**Two beings meet from unequal histories and neither owns the other's account.** The user enters with an actually lived psyche and a deliberately bounded fictional material position; the user does not acquire Nadia's childhood through equalized wages. Nadia sees only behavior she could actually encounter and may mistakenly interpret it. The user notices selectively and may miss what matters to Nadia. Her own self-accounts can be false, partial, strategic, tender or changing. A nearer relationship can increase understanding and misrecognition at the same time. Thus intimacy is a possibility, not the measure of progress.

**The experience is positive before it is pedagogical.** Sometimes the visitor wants to be around Nadia because they enjoy being around Nadia: her way of laughing with a friend, the rhythm they have acquired while working together, the possibility of an argument that neither must win, a familiar greeting that has become their own. And Nadia may or may not desire the same time together, for different reasons, in different degrees, or in incompatible forms. The resulting companionship is a real historical achievement *between* distinct lives; its moments of ease do not erase the unequal cost of time, obligation, expectation, bodily work or conflicting commitments. Their shared practice may become more graceful even as the two remain profoundly mistaken about one another. **The irreducible kernel is neither knowledge as possession nor pleasure as reward, but freely consequential participation in a particular, historically developing relationship whose other center is never the user's creation.**

**The experienced world is neither an explanatory dossier nor an ungrounded story generator.** A book, bill, pair of sandals, knife or shared expression may have material provenance and different subjective meanings. The visitor receives bounded appearance; closer attention can expose further concrete particulars without awarding the actor's hidden motive. An utterance exists as an event even when its proposition is false. The person can retain and revise her memories without rewriting what occurred. One objective history permits different actors' perceptions and competing interpretations.

**The visitor's freedom is real precisely because it is limited by other freedom.** A declared intention is not a completed act; a successful action is not a verified motive; an offer is not guaranteed acceptance. The visitor may become skillful at an ordinary practice while their relation remains unwelcoming, or be cared for without understanding what care costs. An encounter can lead to friendship, love, conflict, helpful co-activity, indifference, injury, rupture or nothing especially consequential. No programmed moral destination corrects these outcomes. Lawful coercion or kindness in the fictional world is not a lesson that the operator must make legible as such.

**The life must remain ordinary enough to be alive.** A simulated adult works, rests, eats, worries, maintains relationships, forgets, recovers, meets others and sometimes does very little that merits a scene. Historical compression must sustain the independent continuity without making every offscreen hour an expensive performance. The visitor's occasional arrival cannot become the organizing dramatic cause of the Other's life. Availability, message, bodily co-presence, refusal and absence remain different practical social possibilities. An intensified encounter must return to tomorrow's material life.

**What changes is a field of future possibilities, not an abstract relationship value.** A damaged object, a promise, an unanswered question, a mistaken inference, a shared practice or a particular injury can alter what each person is next able and willing to do. Some effects are visible; others remain private, displaced, delayed or attributable chiefly to independent actors. A relation can grow more particular without becoming more affectionate. The human may change through experience without Rawlsing possessing the right to define who they have become.

This is the integrated motion, **not a new sequence of engines**: historically constituted world and person → independently continuing activity → situated human entry and finite encounter → actual event and distinct experiences → material and psychological afterlife → new possible encounters, always constrained by what happened and never guaranteed to produce intimacy. The computational structure must reuse actual world/event/organism owners and conserve inherited sensory, provenance, source/permission and replay functions. **NEIGHBOR's task is to make wanting to know somebody an action capable of making history—not to turn the rest of their life into content to unlock.**

The deepest theological and political claim is immanent rather than a victory condition: another life is neither an extension of my will nor an object I can exhaust by knowing it. That claim must remain translatable into real social limits—other people's time, work, needs, interpretive freedom, rights of refusal, and the nonownership of their developing possibilities. It does not appoint the human visitor to liberate her institutions, turn her into a symbol of suffering, or make the system judge their soul.

## 22. The encounter in one continuous motion

A real human accepts a truthful invitation to encounter one unfamiliar, historically existing person. They receive enough orientation to understand what a portal is and where their own powers end; not an encyclopedia of Nadia, a behavioral assessment or a forecast of intimacy. The local material shell makes ordinary prices and opportunities consequential without disclosing Nadia's private finances. A precontact fictional situation may be established, but its authority comes from her independently constituted life, not from the designer's preferred dinner.

The world is already under way. Nadia may be busy, curious, guarded, weary, distracted by somebody else, or absent when the user arrives. Her actual day and past recruit distinct responses. The visitor can try to talk, pay attention, make an offer, act physically, stay silent, leave or come back. They learn through what is said, done, seen, refused, remembered or misinterpreted. They do not issue a 'know Nadia better' instruction and receive more of her history in return.

A shared object might make the mechanism visible. An old book has a real material past. The visitor notices a marked page, asks a question, or offers to help repair its binding. Nadia may welcome the attention, refuse the handling, mistake the visitor's intention or barely care about it. A third person can recognize the book differently. The visitor's interpretation and Nadia's own account need not coincide with its historical provenance. The scene need not reveal a secret; it becomes particular through ordinary specificity and durable consequences.

Later they may cross paths again, communicate through an established channel, discover a shared practice or become less reachable. Nadia's work and relationships have continued; some have changed for reasons not traceable to the user. A previous act may have gained different meaning, or no later meaning at all. The user may have grown perceptually familiar without being loved, been liked without being understood, become important to a third person, or disappeared from the story.

**Nothing in this sequence demands a school-supplies campaign, childcare collective, moral-physics seminar, compulsory dinner, identity quiz, platform donation or engineered revelation.** Those may be imaginable activities in a free world, examples for testing particular subsystems, or separate product-policy questions; they are not NEIGHBOR's objective. The world is needed because a person does not exist solely to answer the human's interest in them.

The narrative becomes more capable not by attaching more complications to the experience but by letting these few laws generate particular, conflicting, unfinished lives. **There is a game because the visitor wants to know someone. There is a world because that someone does not exist to be known.**

## 23. Historical inheritance without greenfield restoration

**This is the current positive NEIGHBOR-UX theory, not a new interpretation of everything the original SOROK source corpus ever considered.** The 23 movements above articulate a bounded, identifiable experience whose center was progressively determined after the original greenfield period and strengthened by direct user corrections. The [cumulative source-draw ledger](13_NEIGHBOR_THEORYMULCH_SOURCE_DRAW_LEDGER.md) preserves historical sources and round-by-round adjudication; the immutable source foundation immediately below preserves their original text and inventories. Neither historical existence nor a late assistant rephrasing is authority to reinstall an early proposal as a governing gameplay objective.

**The outside is explicitly conserved.** The [single OUTSIDE-HOME bank](14_OUTSIDE_HOME_META_PREUX_AND_GREENFIELD_HOLD.md) holds real-human pre-UX distribution, invitations, time-mode packaging, platform economic and data-policy candidates, optional charitable transfers, interface presentation alternatives, research/scientific evaluation proposals and early unselected game forms. These can materially condition the experience, but they do not become in-fiction aims, tasks or actor motivations. A concrete world event such as a labor dispute can happen in Nadia's independent life without making the visitor's game about organizing her workplace.

**Founding and continuing duties remain separated.** The Constitutive Organism is the producer of historically acquired psyche and autonomous life, NEIGHBOR the consumer and bearer of the external human's finite encounter. Real source and code fusions must preserve the inherited world/materialization, perceptual-gaze, situated action, event-binding and psychological afterlife functions without collapsing them into narration. Their current implementation, scientific authority and independent receiving are not proved by the completeness of this manuscript.

**Engineering constraints are obligations, not an aestheticized 'right to fail'.** Generic chatbot behavior, meaningless continuity, fictitious consent, unnatural scripted crises, incoherent access, impossible compute costs and other original alpha negatives are defects the system must be designed to avoid or repair. A bounded working approximation may require deliberate fidelity/cost tradeoffs, but it cannot claim that failure is philosophically desirable. Original hostile cases and unresolved candidate mechanisms stay in the ledger and outside bank until an actual first consumer or renewed user decision makes them material.

The HOME keeps live theoretical contradictions rather than a menu of speculative products. If a historical shard adds a genuinely missing operation or makes the enacted encounter more precise, it belongs in the relevant movement. If it is already represented, the ledger records the duplicate without swelling the prose. If it is a possible surrounding product decision, it belongs outside. If it was superseded, record its historical function without reviving it. The proper end is an **ever more determinate integrated conception of coming to know a person**, not a larger pile of ideas.

The exact source-conservation foundation follows unchanged, and original earlier HOME versions are permanently inspectable at immutable Git commits.

## SOURCE-CONSERVATION FOUNDATION — IN THIS DOCUMENT, NOT A COMPETING THEORY

**Scope and proof limit.** The following entries are recoverable source identities, not 861 independent features and not verified total semantic equivalences. Exact historical Git versions and source IDs are included so an unresolved item cannot be silently lost. Every predecessor has been consulted in this pass through its versioned sections and conservation registers; the relevant original source remains inspectable by exact SHA. This file does not claim to reproduce missing original chat messages, certify source authority, or overwrite protected owners. Items not expressly settled remain **HELD_SOURCE** rather than assumed negated by silence.

### Original SOROK H001–H190:

Original **190/190** September 29 conversation-harvest headings and historic dispositions are retained below. Where the source supplied a separate `Claim:` passage, both original title and specific claim are preserved. Where it did not, the title is **not** inflated into an invented explanatory paragraph. Original full source: `SOROK_PRODUCT_CONTROL_ROOM/12_CHAT_EXHAUSTIVE_HARVEST_AUDIT_R5.md` at `a4211c3ec77e8269097601fe9a3dce2745395ac0`. Source is a historically authored conversation harvest, not a verbatim original-chat export.

| ID | Original heading / assertion | Exact explicit Claim paragraph, where present | Original grade / live semantic hold |
|---|---|---|---|
| H001 | Interaction is not chat-in-a-void | Rawlsing interaction should be situated participation inside a causally live scene in another person's ongoing life. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H002 | Drop the user into the middle of an already-running life | The persona already has pressures, needs, obligations, other relationships, tasks and events before the user acts. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H003 | The persona is not a service agent waiting for input | Other actors may continue cooking, parenting, working, leaving, ignoring, delaying or prioritizing something else. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H004 | Three high-level interaction verbs | The user needs at least SAY, DO and ATTEND. | BANKED_HYPOTHESIS; further independent meaning review open |
| H005 | Natural language is the primary open action surface | The user should be able to type rich embodied/social actions rather than select only canned dialogue responses. | BANKED_HYPOTHESIS; further independent meaning review open |
| H006 | Conventional dialogue-choice menus are not the primary interaction grammar | Structured affordances may exist, but the core experience should not collapse into selecting prewritten response options. | BANKED_HYPOTHESIS; further independent meaning review open |
| H007 | User controls attempts, not reality | The user may specify what they try to say/do/attend to, but not feasibility, success, reception, interpretation or consequence. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H008 | User may specify delivery | Tone attempt, volume, pace, posture, gesture and expression may be part of the attempted action. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H009 | Intended performance is not achieved reception | “I try to sound apologetic” is lawful; “I sound convincingly apologetic to Nadia” is not user-authoritative. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H010 | Compound user prose is a plan, not a screenplay | A multi-step action can fail or be interrupted before later steps occur. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H011 | The user does not own the conversational floor | Another actor can interrupt, walk away, refuse, answer early or change the scene before a long speech completes. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H012 | Some compound actions are genuinely concurrent | Speaking while covertly glancing, gesturing while listening, etc. may overlap rather than serialize. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H013 | No user access to another person's mind | The user cannot directly query hidden affect, motive, private appraisal, hidden memory or relationship score. | BANKED_LAW + INHERITED; further independent meaning review open |
| H014 | The narrator is phenomenological, not psychological | It reports the user's accessible surface of reality. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H015 | Hard show-not-tell | Prefer observable action to labels such as annoyed, nervous, warm, curt or secretly polite. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H016 | If asked “is she annoyed?”, return evidence, not hidden-state confirmation | The narrator may restate behavior/speech but does not adjudicate the mind. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H017 | No exaggerated behavioral tells for player convenience | Hidden state should cause behavior through the persona's actual organization; the system should not manufacture obvious clues so the player can solve them. | INHERITED + BANKED_HYPOTHESIS; further independent meaning review open |
| H018 | Full inhibition without leakage is possible | Not every hidden motive must leak into observable behavior. | INHERITED; further independent meaning review open |
| H019 | Leakage, when present, need not look like a cliché tell | It may appear as verbosity, topic return, avoidance, attentional asymmetry, humor, anecdote choice, delay, etc. | INHERITED; further independent meaning review open |
| H020 | Narrator is non-curatorial | It cannot select a detail because hidden system state knows the detail is psychologically important. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H021 | Narrator selection may use perceptual salience | Finite output can use proximity, motion, sensory intensity, ordinary scene organization and explicit attention target. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H022 | Ambient scene frame should provide obvious orientation | Surface-only narration should not force the user to ask whether a table or visible child exists. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H023 | Ambient perception and focused ATTEND are distinct | Immediate coarse orientation is cheap/ongoing; focused inspection acquires deeper resolution. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H024 | Progressive perceptual resolution | Fridge → bill → exact bill text should require increasingly specific attention/access. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H025 | ATTEND cannot silently move/manipulate the user | If the bill is unreadable from here, the narrator cannot walk the user closer. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H026 | User can continue inspecting while reality allows | No arbitrary “three investigations per scene” quota. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H027 | User mention does not create world fact | Saying “Percocet” does not instantiate Percocet. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H028 | Narrated objective facts become binding persistent reality | If the narrator lawfully says a chipped green mug exists, it exists until causal change removes/alters it. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H029 | Internal implementation should admit fact before narrating it | World completion/retrieval precedes narration so prose cannot become accidental world authority. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H030 | Leading questions cannot summon convenient reality | “Is that a shutoff notice?” does not make it one. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H031 | Unmaterialized implementation detail is not diegetic absence | A drawer can have existed with contents before the system needed to compute them. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H032 | Lazy completion must be history-compatible | New detail cannot contradict prior use, narration, memory, ownership or material constraint. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H033 | First computational materialization time != in-world creation time | An old magnet first resolved on day 30 may have been present for years. | BANKED_HYPOTHESIS / implementation requirement; further independent meaning review open |
| H034 | World specificity may be forced by other actors, not only user curiosity | Nadia using an object can require its completion before the user ever sees it. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H035 | Mundane objects can be dense evidence | Bills, menus, school schedules and magnets can reveal material/social history if inspected specifically. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |
| H036 | Evidence is not authorial clue placement | The fridge contains those objects because the household's life produced them, not to deliver plot information to the player. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |

| H041 | Different observations take different amounts of time | Room glance, fridge scan, exact bill reading and exhaustive object inspection have different durations. | BANKED_LAW; further independent meaning review open |

### Direct user corrections that override otherwise appealing prior assistant formulations

1. **Exact same socioeconomic situation at first entry**, not vaguely similar class; the equivalence is not narrated and the visitor does **not** know Nadia's financial balances. Only their own resource-cost preview is given. Later asymmetric change is not announced or re-equalized.
2. **Opening from Nadia's actual world condition that day**, with plausible meeting routes based on her commitments, disposition, skills and Others. An inexhaustible catalog of bespoke hooks is not a settled requirement. Shared reference-day continuation is a proposal pending existing ownership.
3. **Opening constitutes bounded consent to a mundane fictional prelude**; the categorical ban on previously exchanged words, a rescheduled invitation or a harmless prior acceptance is superseded. This cannot be expanded into unattended future free-agent speech for the human.
4. **The positive user pleasure is getting to know someone and perhaps getting closer**; this is distinct from any simulator obligation to optimize Nadia for relational access or to avoid closeness in order to demonstrate otherness.
5. **No invention of purportedly missing source machinery.** Mechanism I Birkenstocks materialization, the existing encounter and phenomenal owners, PRE-A0, PYAT G4, CO and real implementation witnesses must be consulted before an architecture proposal.
6. **One document, one conceptual whole.** Previous per-round files and machine no-drop registers can remain *historical evidence* until a source-safe deletion is verified; they must not become competing current theories. The exact earlier original chat text is still not fully recovered, which limits any literal guarantee that every unrecorded assent was captured.
